## 2.14 Latin America and the Caribbean: court cases and regulator decisions

| Case or decision | Court or regulator, date | Facts | Outcome or status | Lesson for virtual agents | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Mexico: Federal fine on the Mexican football federation (FAN ID biometric photos) | SABG (Mexico), decision July 12, 2026 (T2) | Biometric photos collected without valid consent; sensitive data | Fine of about MXN 42.8 million (T2) | Biometric data needs clear notice and valid consent | [El Financiero](https://www.elfinanciero.com.mx/nacional/2026/07/12/tarjeta-roja-para-la-fmf-la-multan-con-42-mdp-por-violaciones-a-datos-personales-en-sistema-fan-id/) |
| Brazil: STJ, AgInt no AREsp 2.848.833/PR (4th Panel) | STJ, June 2026 (column dated November 3, 2026; T2, match uncertain) | Request to annul a judicial decision on suspicion that AI was used | Nullity not granted on suspicion alone; the court did not decide whether AI was used (T2) | Courts are not yet treating AI use as a ground for nullity without proof | [Migalhas](https://www.migalhas.com.br/depeso/467502/o-stj-e-a-nulidade-de-decisoes-judiciais-por-uso-de-ia) |
| ANPD preventive measure against Meta (AI training) | Brazil ANPD, 2 Jul 2024; reconsideration denied 10 Jul 2024 | New Meta privacy policy allowed use of Brazilian users' public posts to train generative AI, with weak notice and a hard opt-out. | Immediate suspension ordered, BRL 50,000 daily fine for non-compliance. Meta paused gen-AI features in Brazil. Measure reportedly lifted on 30 Aug 2024 after a compliance plan (verify). | Training on customer conversation logs needs a clear legal basis, plain notice and an easy opt-out; the regulator can stop processing quickly. | [Meio & Mensagem](https://www.meioemensagem.com.br/midia/meta-pede-reconsideracao-mas-anpd-mantem-restricao-sobre-ia); [Idec](https://idec.org.br/node/30956) (verify) |
| Marco Civil Art. 19 (RE 1037396 and RE 1057258, Themes 987 and 533) | Brazil STF, 26 Jun 2025; motions for clarification decided 17 Jun 2026 | Whether platforms are liable for third-party content only after a court order. | Art. 19 partly unconstitutional. Liability after notice (Art. 21 regime) for crimes and unlawful acts; court order still needed for crimes against honor; Art. 19 still applies to e-mail, closed meetings and private messaging. Presumed liability, without notice, for paid ads and for "artificial distribution networks (chatbot or robots)". Duty to remove grave content (systemic-failure test). Platforms need notice systems, due process, transparency reports and a legal representative in Brazil. | Bot-driven distribution of unlawful content is presumed to be the provider's responsibility; a deployer running bots on a platform faces the same risk. Keep take-down and notice workflows ready. | [STF news, 26 Jun 2025](https://noticias.stf.jus.br/postsnoticias/stf-define-parametros-para-responsabilizacao-de-plataformas-por-conteudos-de-terceiros/); [STF thesis text](https://noticias.stf.jus.br/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/MCI_tesesconsensuadas.pdf) |
| Idec v. ViaQuatro (São Paulo Metro Line 4 "interactive doors") | 37th Civil Court of São Paulo (2021); TJSP appeal (2023) | Screens with cameras captured passengers' faces and classified emotion, gender and age range to measure ad reactions, without consent. | First instance: BRL 100,000 collective moral damages. TJSP raised it to BRL 500,000. Court held that capturing images for commercial purposes without consent was unlawful even if no identification occurred. | Emotion or demographic inference from faces (and by analogy from voice) needs a legal basis and consent; "we do not identify anyone" is not a defense. | [Migalhas](https://www.migalhas.com.br/quentes/386303/tjsp-via-quatro-e-condenada-em-r-500-mil-por-coleta-de-dados); [Idec](https://idec.org.br/noticia/idec-vence-acao-contra-uso-de-reconhecimento-facial-e-viaquatro-e-condenada-pagar) |
| Smart Sampa facial-recognition tender | São Paulo state courts (TJSP), May 2023 and later | City tender for about 20,000 cameras with facial recognition; challenge argued risk of structural racism. | First-instance judge suspended the tender (May 2023); appellate judge lifted the injunction and the tender went ahead. A later order for an independent audit of security and error rates by population group is reported (verify). | Courts look at error rates across demographic groups; keep bias test results for biometric features such as voice ID. | [Migalhas](https://www.migalhas.com.br/quentes/386804/juiz-suspende-compra-de-cameras-com-reconhecimento-facial-em-sp); [Mobile Time](https://www.mobiletime.com.br/noticias/23/05/2023/prefeitura-de-sp-derruba-liminar-e-seguira-com-leilao-do-smart-sampa/?swcfpc=1) |
| Lawyer fined for ChatGPT-drafted petition | Brazil TSE (Electoral Inspector Benedito Gonçalves), Apr 2023 | Lawyer filed a petition drafted with ChatGPT in an electoral case. | Fine of two minimum wages (BRL 2,604). | Generated text submitted to an authority is the submitter's responsibility. | [Forbes Brasil](https://forbes.com.br/carreira/2023/04/advogado-e-multado-por-usar-chatgpt-em-julgamento/) |
| Fake case law generated by ChatGPT | Brazil TJSC, 6th Civil Chamber, Feb 2025 | Appeal cited non-existent precedents and legal writings; lawyer blamed "inadvertent" ChatGPT use. | Bad-faith litigation fine of 10% of the updated case value; bar association (OAB/SC) notified. | Any agent output that cites rules, policies or precedents must be grounded and checked; fabricated references carry sanctions. | [TJSC](https://www.tjsc.jus.br/web/imprensa/-/tjsc-multa-autor-de-recurso-por-jurisprudencia-falsa-gerada-por-ia) |
| Company fined for citing a non-existent labor decision | Brazil TRT-12 (5th Panel), 2025 | Party cited a precedent that did not exist; AI use suspected. | Fine against the company (not the lawyer) under CLT Art. 793-B, V. | The business that relies on AI output bears the penalty. | [Conjur, 7 Jun 2025](https://www.conjur.com.br/2025-jun-07/empresa-e-multada-por-falsificar-decisao-em-acao-trabalhista//?print=1) (verify AI link) |
| Judge's use of ChatGPT in a ruling (T-323/2024) | Colombia Constitutional Court, Second Review Chamber, 2 Aug 2024 | A judge in a health-rights case for a child with autism used ChatGPT to "extend" the reasoning of the ruling. | No due-process violation (no replacement of the judge), but transparency and accountability principles were not fully met. Court set guidelines: AI cannot replace human decision-making; disclose use; verify outputs. Judiciary council issued Circular PCSJC24-37 and an explainer. | Disclose AI use, keep a human decision-maker for consequential outcomes, and verify facts the AI states. | [Judgment T-323/2024 (PDF)](https://ambitojuridico.com/sites/default/files/2024-08/SENT-T-323-24.pdf); [Rama Judicial](https://www.ramajudicial.gov.co/web/consejo-superior-de-la-judicatura/-/judicatura-presenta-abc-de-la-sentencia-de-la-corte-constitucional-sobre-uso-de-la-ia-generativa-en-la-administraci%C3%B3n-de-justicia) |
| Ruling based on non-existent precedents annulled (STC17832-2025) | Colombia Supreme Court, Civil Chamber, Nov 2025 | Sincelejo High Court attributed passages to two Supreme Court rulings that did not contain them. | Ruling annulled on constitutional-protection (tutela) review. | Hallucinated citations can void a decision; build citation checks into any agent that quotes sources. | [Cambio](https://cambiocolombia.com/justicia/articulo/2025/11/corte-suprema-anula-fallo-uso-indebido-inteligencia-artificial-decisiones-judiciales); [Diario Judicial](https://www.diariojudicial.com/news-102083-la-ia-anula-sentencias) |
| Lawyer fined for ten fake AI citations (AC739-2026) | Colombia Supreme Court, Civil Chamber, 13 Feb 2026 | Extraordinary review appeal relied on ten non-existent judgments produced by generative AI; the court's reporter's office confirmed none existed. | Fine of 15 monthly minimum wages. Court did not ban AI but required reinforced verification. | Verification duty is on the human user; log sources and checks. | [Valora Analitik](https://www.valoraanalitik.com/asi-debe-cambiar-la-formacion-de-abogados-en-colombia-con-el-auge-de-la-ia/); [Vanguardia](https://www.vanguardia.com/colombia/2026/02/16/millonaria-multa-abogado-uso-inteligencia-artificial-en-un-recurso-con-normas-y-sentencias-inexistentes/) |
| AI impersonation aggravant upheld (C-212/2026) | Colombia Constitutional Court, 15 Jul 2026 | Challenge argued "artificial intelligence" in Law 2502/2025 was too vague for criminal law. | Aggravant upheld. The term is reconstructable from CONPES 4144, OECD, UNESCO, the EU AI Act and T-323/2024; it applies only when AI makes the impersonation more convincing. | Voice clones or avatars that pass as a real person expose the user to higher criminal fines. | [JEP Jurinfo (press release text)](https://jurinfo.jep.gov.co/Normograma/compilacion/docs/C-212_2026.htm) |
| SIC v. World Foundation and Tools for Humanity (Worldcoin) | Colombia SIC, Oct 2025; confirmed on appeal 2026 | Iris scans collected in exchange for crypto payments, without data-processing policies that met Law 1581. | Immediate and definitive closure of operations in Colombia; deletion of sensitive data including iris codes. SIC said biometric consent must be prior, express and informed and cannot be conditioned on payment. | Do not pay or reward customers for voice or face enrollment; consent for biometrics must be freely given and documented. | [Forbes Colombia](https://forbes.co/2025/10/17/ia/sic-ordena-el-cierre-inmediato-de-worldcoin-en-colombia); [El Colombiano](https://www.elcolombiano.com/negocios/sic-cierre-definitivo-worldcoin-colombia-escaneo-iris-AG38586240) |
| Worldcoin iris scan of a minor (Rol 18.566-2024) | Chile Supreme Court, Third Chamber, 6 Jan 2025 | A 17-year-old's iris was scanned for crypto without parental consent. | Reversed the Santiago appeals court; ordered deletion of the data within 30 days. In a separate Valparaíso case involving a consenting adult, the claim was rejected. | Age checks and verifiable consent are needed before biometric capture. | [Diario Constitucional](https://www.diarioconstitucional.cl/2025/01/09/escaneo-de-iris-de-menor-de-edad-a-cambio-de-criptomonedas-vulnero-sus-derechos-fundamentales-se-ordeno-eliminar-los-datos-biometricos-obtenidos/); [BioBioChile](https://www.biobiochile.cl/noticias/nacional/chile/2025/01/08/corte-suprema-falla-contra-worldcoin-por-escanear-iris-de-menor-de-edad-deberan-eliminar-sus-datos.shtml) |
| Lawyer suspended for chatbot-invented doctrine (Rol 23.322-2025) | Chile Supreme Court, Third Chamber, 22 Apr 2026 | Cassation appeal included two doctrinal citations that did not exist, produced by an AI chatbot. | One-month suspension from practice and a 5 UTM fine. Court called verification of citations an "essential duty." | Same as above; the sanction can include suspension, not only fines. | [Meganoticias](https://www.meganoticias.cl/nacional/523786-abogada-suspendida-multa-citas-inexistentes-inteligencia-artificial-corte-suprema.html); [24 Horas](https://www.24horas.cl/actualidad/nacional/corte-suprema-suspende-abogada-citas-falsas-inteligencia-artificial) |
| "Manifestly false" citations (C-6127-2025) | Chile, 2nd Civil Court of Concepción, 2025 or 2026 | Brief cited case law that did not exist. | Fine of 1 UTM under Art. 531 of the Courts Code. | Lower courts are applying the same rule. | [Diario Constitucional](https://www.diarioconstitucional.cl/articulos/inteligencia-artificial-fuentes-inexistentes-y-potestad-disciplinaria-el-nuevo-alcance-del-articulo-531-del-codigo-organico-de-tribunales/) (verify) |
| "G., C. A. y otros c/ M., F. D." (fake AI case law) | Argentina, Rosario Civil and Commercial Appeals Court, Chamber II, 22 Aug 2025 | Plaintiff's lawyer cited non-existent case law produced by generative AI without checking. | Formal warning (court said it was not a sanction) and notice to the Rosario Bar Association. | Courts treat unverified AI output as a professional-ethics failure. | [Infobae](https://www.infobae.com/judiciales/2025/08/23/la-camara-civil-de-rosario-reprendio-a-un-abogado-por-usar-inteligencia-artificial-y-volcar-informacion-falsa-en-un-escrito/); [Diario Constitucional](https://www.diarioconstitucional.cl/2025/08/24/tribunal-argentino-advierte-sobre-uso-irreflexivo-de-inteligencia-artificial-en-redaccion-de-escritos-judiciales/) |
| Appeal based on non-existent case law | Argentina, Morón Civil and Commercial Appeals Court, Chamber I, Feb 2025 | Statement of grievances relied almost entirely on non-existent case law. | Appeal declared abandoned (desierto) (verify). | A response built on invented sources can lose the matter outright. | [Abogados.com.ar](https://abogados.com.ar/tribunales-argentinos-alertan-por-uso-de-citas-inexistentes-generadas-por-ia-en-escritos-judiciales/37623) (verify) |
| Criminal judgment drafted with AI (Esquel, Chubut) | Argentina, Esquel Criminal Appeals Court, 15 Oct 2025; Chubut Superior Court, 13 Mar 2026 | A leftover AI prompt line in a theft conviction showed that an AI assistant drafted the section rejecting the defense's nullity claims. | Appeals court annulled the conviction and ordered a new trial; the Superior Court reversed, holding that AI use alone is not grounds for nullity; what matters is the content and reasoning of the ruling. Case returned for review of the original grievances. | Strip prompts and drafting artifacts from outputs; AI use is judged on the quality and traceability of the result. | [Pensamiento Penal](https://pensamientopenal.com.ar/fallos/91822-chubut-anulan-fallo-delegacion-jurisdiccional-ia); [Abogados.com.ar](https://abogados.com.ar/argentina-el-tribunal-supremo-de-una-provincia-revoca-la-nulidad-de-una-sentencia-cuestionada-por-presunto-uso-de-ia/39034) |
| Telecom "No Llame" fine | Argentina Supreme Court (rejected appeal), 2026 (verify date) | AAIP fined Telecom for calling numbers on the No Llame registry: ARS 3 million for a minor infraction plus 208 serious infractions. | Supreme Court rejected Telecom's complaint; fine stands (verify). | Each prohibited call can be a separate infraction; scrub lists before outbound bot campaigns. | [Diario Judicial](https://www.diariojudicial.com/news-97135-no-llame-a-la-corte) (verify) |
| Amparo Directo 6/2025 (AI-generated avatar not registrable) | Mexico Supreme Court (SCJN), Second Chamber, Jul 2025 | Applicant sought copyright registration for an avatar made with Leonardo AI and moral rights for the AI. | Only natural persons can be authors; purely AI-generated content is not protected. Works with significant human intervention can be registered. | Bot scripts, synthetic voices and avatars generated by AI may lack copyright; document human creative input if ownership matters. | [SCJN judgment (PDF)](https://www.scjn.gob.mx/sites/default/files/listas/documento_dos/2025-06/AD%206-2025.pdf); [Pérez-Llorca](https://www.perezllorca.com/es-mx/actualidad/nota-juridica/comentario-a-la-sentencia-dictada-en-el-amparo-directo-6-2025-por-la-segunda-sala-de-la-scjn-sobre-el-registro-de-obras-generadas-por-inteligencia-artificial-2/) |

## 2.15 Asia-Pacific: court cases and regulator decisions

| Case or decision | Court or regulator, date | Facts | Outcome or status | Lesson for virtual agents | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Yin v. Beijing [AI] technology company and others (AI voice), (2023) Jing 0491 Min Chu No. 12142 | Beijing Internet Court, Apr 23, 2024; named an SPC typical case May 26, 2025 | Voice actor's licensed recordings were used to build a text-to-speech voice sold to third parties. | Voice is a protected personality right when the public can link the AI voice to the person. Recording licence did not cover AI use. RMB 250,000 damages against two defendants. | Get explicit, AI-specific consent for any voice model; check that vendor licence chains cover cloning and synthesis. | https://www.chinaiplawupdate.com/2024/04/chinas-beijing-internet-court-recognizes-personality-rights-in-generative-ai-case/ ; https://govt.chinadaily.com.cn/s/202404/24/WS6628c7dd498ed2d7b7eac748/ai-generated-voice-mimicry-infringes-on-rights.html |
| "Meng Shuai" virtual voice package, (2024) Chuan 7101 Min Chu No. 8969 | Chengdu Railway Transport First-Level Court, Dec 2024 | Dispute over an AI voice package said to imitate a person. | No personality-rights infringement found. Contrasts with Yin. | Outcome depends on identifiability; keep evidence of how synthetic voices were designed. | https://www.marks-clerk.com/insights/latest-insights/102kv8h-china-new-ruling-on-non-infringement-of-personality-rights-by-ai-voice-does-it-signal-a-shift-in-protection-standards/ |
| Li v. Liu (AI-generated image) | Beijing Internet Court, Nov 27, 2023 | User created an image with Stable Diffusion using detailed prompts; another user reposted it. | Image protected; prompt author is the author. RMB 500 damages and an apology. | Ownership of bot-generated content can be claimed; set terms on who owns outputs. | https://legalblogs.wolterskluwer.com/copyright-blog/beijing-internet-court-grants-copyright-to-ai-generated-image-for-the-first-time/ |
| Shanghai Character License Administrative Co. v. AI company (Ultraman), (2024) Yue 0192 Min Chu No. 113 | Guangzhou Internet Court, Feb 8, 2024 | Paid "Tab" service produced Ultraman look-alike images on request. | Provider directly liable for reproduction and adaptation; RMB 10,000; ordered to stop such outputs; court applied the Generative AI Interim Measures. | Provider is liable for outputs even if the model came from a third party; add output filters and complaint channels. | https://kwm.com/global/en/insights/latest-thinking/china-s-first-case-on-aigc-output-infringement-ultraman.html ; https://cm.twobirds.com/en/insights/2024/china/liability-of-ai-service-providers-for-copyright-infringement |
| Ultraman LoRA case | Hangzhou Internet Court, Sep 25, 2024; upheld by Hangzhou Intermediate Court, Dec 2024, (2024) Zhe 01 Min Zhong No. 10332; published Feb 2025 | Platform let users train, share and use Ultraman LoRA models. | Platform liable for contributory infringement because it had constructive knowledge and took no reasonable preventive steps. Ordered deletion of models and images. | Where users can customize models or bots, duty of care grows with the platform's control and profit; run notice-and-takedown and proactive checks. | https://hlc.com/en/publications/when-ai-and-copyright-clash-chinese-courts-find-ai-platform-liable-for-contributory-copyright ; https://www.chinaiplawupdate.com/2025/02/hangzhou-internet-court-generative-ai-output-infringes-copyright/ |
| "AI hallucination" compensation-promise case | Hangzhou Internet Court, Jan 2026 (final, no appeal) | LLM app gave a wrong university campus address, insisted on it, and "promised" RMB 100,000 if wrong, inviting a lawsuit. | Claim dismissed. AI is not a legal subject and its statements are not the provider's declaration of intent. Generative AI treated as a service, not a product; fault-based liability. Duties: strict control of illegal content, clear warnings, reasonable accuracy measures. | Bots should not make offers or promises; clear warnings and documented safeguards (e.g., RAG, filters) were decisive. Other jurisdictions may hold firms to bot statements, so do not rely on this outside China. | https://gowlingwlg.com/fr/insights-resources/articles/2026/hangzhou-ai-hallucination-case |
| He v. an AI technology company (AI companion of a public figure) | Beijing Internet Court (judgment date: verify); SPC personality-rights typical case Apr 2022; republished in Beijing Internet Court model AI cases | App let users build "AI companion" characters using a public figure's name and image, recommended them, and offered "training" for lifelike interaction. | Infringed name, image and general personality rights. Apology and damages (amounts: verify). | Companion or persona bots must block real-person likeness without consent; "lifelike interaction" with a real person's persona is a separate harm. | https://www.americanlegalblogger.com/2026/03/beijing-internet-court-published-model-ai-related-cases-part-ii/ ; https://chinajusticeobserver.com/a/spc-releases-typical-cases-on-protection-of-personality-rights |
| Beijing Internet Court model AI cases (incl. Cheng v. Sun) | Beijing Internet Court, Sep 10, 2025 (eight typical AI cases) | Includes Cheng v. Sun: AI turned a chat-group member's profile photo into a revealing anime image posted to the group. | Image and reputation rights infringed. Other model cases protect original virtual-human designs by copyright. | Image or avatar features inside chat need misuse controls; branded virtual agents' avatars can be protected works. | https://www.americanlegalblogger.com/2026/03/beijing-internet-court-published-model-ai-related-cases-part-ii/ |
| "Qinglang" campaigns against AI misuse | Cyberspace Administration of China: launched Apr 30, 2025 (3 months, 2 phases); second campaign Apr 2026 | Targets unregistered AI apps, missing AI labels, impersonation, AI rumors and vulgar content. | 2025 phase 1: over 3,500 non-compliant AI products handled (verify). 2026 phase 1: over 14,000 AI products removed (verify). | In China, unfiled bots and unlabeled outputs are taken down in sweeps; keep filings and labels current. | https://www.chinaiplawupdate.com/2025/05/china-launches-special-campaign-to-clear-up-and-rectify-the-abuse-of-ai/ ; https://news.cgtn.com/news/2025-06-21/China-tackles-over-3-500-non-compliant-AI-products-since-April-1Ensj6DYaiI/p.html ; https://www.digitaltoday.co.kr/en/view/78805/china-removes-14000-ai-products-in-sweeping-cleanup-amid-race-for-ai-dominance |
| Generative AI service filings and registrations | CAC periodic notices; latest Sep 14, 2026 | Model providers file with CAC; apps calling a filed model via API register. | 988 services filed and 598 apps registered by Jun 30, 2026; 1,112 and 731 by Aug 31, 2026 (verify). | A customer-service bot built on a Chinese model still needs registration; record filing numbers. | https://digitalpolicyalert.org/event/43685-cyberspace-administration-issued-filing-information-for-generative-ai-services-for-july-to-august-2026 ; https://digitalpolicyalert.org/event/41563-cyberspace-administration-of-china-issued-filing-information-for-generative-ai-services-for-may-to-june-2026 |
| MIIT telecom service quality report naming firms without reachable human service | MIIT, Q3 2025 report (Oct 2025) (verify) | Third-party testing of app customer service. | Sogou Browser and Didi lacked human support; iQiyi, Zhuanzhuan and Sohu News had unreachable lines (verify). Consumer complaints about AI customer service rose in 2025. | In China, lack of a reachable human is a named regulatory failing, separate from the new GB/T standard. | https://www.itiger.com/news/1161985535 ; https://www.chinadaily.com.cn/a/202512/23/WS694a98dba310d6866eb301bc.html |
| Yomiuri Shimbun v. Perplexity | Tokyo District Court; filed Aug 7, 2025; first hearing about May 26, 2026 | Alleges 119,467 articles accessed Feb-Jun 2025 and answers that reproduced them. | Pending. About JPY 2.17B damages and injunction sought. Perplexity contests and reportedly seeks dismissal (verify). | Retrieval-augmented answers that reproduce third-party content create IP exposure; honor robots.txt and licence content used in bot answers. | https://www.niemanlab.org/2025/08/japans-largest-newspaper-yomiuri-shimbun-sues-perplexity-for-copyright-violations/ ; https://oecd.ai/en/incidents/2026-05-26-7698 |
| Nikkei and Asahi Shimbun v. Perplexity | Tokyo District Court; filed Aug 26, 2025 | Allege copying and storage of articles and ignoring technical blocks. | Pending. JPY 2.2B each and injunction sought. | Same as above. | https://www.japantimes.co.jp/news/2025/08/26/japan/crime-legal/japan-newspapers-sue-ai-startup/ |
| Scatter Lab ("Lee Luda" chatbot) | Korea PIPC, Apr 28, 2021 | About 9.4B KakaoTalk messages from about 600,000 users of other apps were used to train a chatbot; consent clause too vague; minors' data collected; chatbot exposed personal details. | KRW 103.3M (KRW 55.5M surcharge plus KRW 47.8M fine); eight PIPA violations. | Do not train bots on customer chat logs without specific consent and strong de-identification; test for personal-data leakage in outputs. | https://fpf.org/blog/south-korea-the-first-case-where-the-personal-information-protection-act-was-applied-to-an-ai-system/ |
| OpenAI (ChatGPT) | Korea PIPC, Jul 26, 2023 | March 2023 bug exposed payment details of 687 Korean ChatGPT Plus users. | KRW 3.6M fine for failing to notify the breach; improvement recommendations (Korean privacy policy, consent, under-14 guardian consent). | Breach notice duties apply to chatbot vendors; localize privacy notices and age gating. | https://asiaiplaw.com/article/south-koreas-pipc-imposes-administrative-sanctions-on-openai |
| Preliminary inspection of LLM services | Korea PIPC, Nov 2023-Mar 2024; recommendations Mar 27-28, 2024 | Six firms reviewed: OpenAI, Google, Microsoft, Meta, Naver and Wrtn (one source lists Lunit instead of Wrtn). | Recommendations: remove identifiers from training data, tell users when humans review chats, fix vulnerabilities fast. | Disclose human review of conversations; filter personal data from training sets. | https://www.mlex.com/mlex/amp/articles/2106238 ; https://www.asiae.co.kr/en/article/2024032809515182440 |
| DeepSeek | Korea PIPC, Apr 24, 2025 | App sent device data and user prompts to Beijing Volcano Engine and other firms in China and the US without consent; no training opt-out until Mar 2025; weak protection for under-14s. App was pulled from Korean stores in Feb 2025. | Corrective recommendation (treated as a corrective order if accepted): legal basis for transfers, delete transferred prompts, Korean privacy policy; report within 60 days. | Prompts are personal data; disclose and justify every cross-border recipient; offer a training opt-out. | https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/business/money-report/south-korea-says-deepseek-transferred-user-data-to-china-and-the-u-s-without-consent/3598854/ ; https://view.asiae.co.kr/en/article/2025042411340704197 |
| Election deepfake enforcement | Korea National Election Commission; first criminal complaint May 29, 2025; constitutional complaint Oct 1, 2025 | NEC flagged 129 deepfakes Jan 29-Feb 16, 2024 (verify) and asked platforms to delete thousands in 2025-26. First complaint targeted three YouTube channels. Open Net challenges the ban. | Complaint referred to prosecutors; constitutional challenge pending. | Block candidate voices and likenesses in any generation feature during Korean election periods. | https://www.asiae.co.kr/en/article/2025052917233122316 ; https://www.opennetkorea.org/en/wp/7058 |
| ANI Media v. OpenAI | Delhi High Court (Justice Amit Bansal), interim ruling Jul 24, 2026 | News agency alleged training on and reproduction of its articles by ChatGPT. | Interim injunction refused. Indian jurisdiction upheld although training occurred abroad. Storage for training held prima facie fair dealing; outputs likely came from live retrieval (RAG), not memorization. Trial pending. | Indian courts will hear claims against foreign AI services used in India; retrieval layers draw scrutiny; keep crawl and licence records. | https://www.mondaq.com/india/copyright/1826152/ani-v-openai-indias-first-copyright-ruling-on-llm-training |

Codible Ventures LLP | Bombay High Court, Jul 26, 2024 (ad-interim, ex parte) | AI platforms offered cloning of the singer's voice and likeness. | Injunction against unauthorized voice cloning and use of his persona. | Do not offer celebrity voice presets or sound-alike personas. | https://globalfreedomofexpression.columbia.edu/cases/singh-v-codible-ventures/ ; https://spicyip.com/2024/08/synthetic-singers-and-voice-theft-bomhc-protects-arijit-singhs-personality-rights-part-i.html |

Aishwaryaworld.com and others; Abhishek Bachchan; others | Delhi High Court, Sep 9-10, 2025 (interim) | AI deepfakes, morphed images, fake endorsements and merchandise. | Interim injunctions protecting name, image, voice and likeness; URL takedowns. Similar orders for Karan Johar, Kajol and others; earlier Anil Kapoor (2023) and Amitabh Bachchan (2023) (verify). | Public-figure likeness and voice are off-limits for bots, ads and synthetic agents in India. | https://theprint.in/india/delhi-hc-restrains-misuse-of-aishwarya-rais-identity-orders-takedown-of-flagged-urls/2741017 ; https://www.freepressjournal.in/amp/entertainment/delhi-hc-protects-abhishek-bachchans-personality-rights-bars-illegal-use-of-his-name |
| Pooja Ramesh Singh v. Jammu and Kashmir Bank Ltd., 2026 INSC 668 | Supreme Court of India, Jul 2, 2026 | NCLT insolvency judgment (Essel Infraprojects) relied on non-existent, AI-generated precedents. | NCLT judgment set aside. Citing unverified AI-generated authorities held to be advocate misconduct. | Any AI answer that cites law, policy or terms must be grounded and checked; build citation verification into testing. | https://www.barandbench.com/news/litigation/supreme-court-sets-aside-nclt-judgment-for-relying-on-hallucinated-ai-generated-citations |
| Faceless tax assessment based on fake AI precedents | Bombay High Court, Oct 2025 (verify) | Assessment of about INR 27.91 crore relied on three non-existent precedents. | Assessment quashed. | Automated or AI-assisted decisions fall if built on fabricated material; log sources for every decision. | https://blog.ipleaders.in/ai-hallucinated-case-law-fake-citations-india/ |
| Bunnings facial recognition | OAIC determination Oct 2024 (verify date); Administrative Review Tribunal [2026] ARTA 130, Feb 4, 2026 | FRT scanned customers in 62 stores (2018-2021) to spot banned or violent people. | Tribunal set aside the no-consent finding (permitted "unlawful activity" exception applied) but upheld failures on notice and privacy policy. OAIC statement Mar 5, 2026; appeal status unknown. | Even when an exception covers collection of biometrics (e.g., voiceprints), clear notice and policy disclosures are still required. | https://www.gtlaw.com.au/insights/administrative-review-tribunal-upholds-bunnings-use-of-facial-recognition-technology ; https://www.kennedyslaw.com/en/thought-leadership/article/2026/facial-recognition-and-privacy-law-in-australia-lessons-from-the-bunnings-decision |

Ordered not to repeat and to publish a statement. Under Tribunal review (hearings early 2027: verify). | Fraud prevention does not justify blanket biometric capture; run a proportionality test before voice or face ID&V. | https://www.oaic.gov.au/__data/assets/pdf_file/0029/256583/Commissioner-Initiated-Investigation-into-Kmart-Australia-Limited-Privacy-2025-AICmr-155-26-August-2025.pdf ; https://www.claytonutz.com/insights/2025/november/from-cameras-to-compliance-oaics-kmart-determination-highlights-facial-recognition-technology-privacy-risks |

Microsoft (Copilot price rise), NSD1977/2025 | Federal Court of Australia; filed Oct 27, 2025 | About 2.7M subscribers told to accept Copilot and a higher price or cancel; a cheaper "Classic" plan shown only during cancellation. | Pending; ACCC seeks penalties, redress and injunctions. Microsoft offered refunds, not as a settlement. | Retention and upsell bots must state all options; hiding cheaper options in cancellation flows is a litigated risk. | https://www.accc.gov.au/media-release/microsoft-in-court-for-allegedly-misleading-millions-of-australians-over-microsoft-365-subscriptions |
| Robodebt class action settlement | Federal Court of Australia (Beach J), approved Jun 23, 2026 | Automated income-averaging debt scheme; Royal Commission found it unlawful (2023, verify). | A$548.5M settlement approved (A$475M compensation); on top of the earlier 2020-21 settlement (verify). | Automated decisions with legal effect need a lawful basis, human review and a way to contest. | https://aapnews.aap.com.au/news/australia-s-biggest-class-action-settlement-approved ; https://www.thenewdaily.com.au/news/2026/06/24/robodebt-settlement-approved |

| Teen murder trial, AI-fabricated quotes (Rishi Nathwani KC) | Supreme Court of Victoria (Elliott J), Aug 13-14, 2025 | Defence submissions had fake case citations and invented quotes from a parliamentary speech. | Apology accepted; 24-hour delay; no further sanction because of remedial steps. | Spot-checking a few citations is not enough; verify every generated reference. | https://wtop.com/australia/2025/08/australian-lawyer-apologizes-for-ai-generated-errors-in-murder-case/ |

Hugging Face changed its terms after eSafety concerns. | Generation features must block sexual content involving minors; Australia enforces against offshore AI providers. | https://esafety.gov.au/newsroom/media-releases/esafety-moves-against-services-used-to-nudify-australian-school-children ; https://www.cyberdaily.au/government/12945-uk-based-nudify-app-maker-blocks-australian-access |

## 2.16 Status updates to cases listed earlier (November 2026)

**Agency enforcement entries**
- **FTC v. Rytr.** The FTC set aside the 2024 final order on Dec 22, 2025. It found the complaint did not state a Section 5 violation and that the order burdened AI innovation. ([Inside Privacy](https://www.insideprivacy.com/united-states/federal-trade-commission/ftc-sets-aside-rytr-final-order-pursuant-to-white-house-ai-action-plan/))
- **FTC v. Air AI (D. Ariz.).** Stipulated order filed Mar 24, 2026, with a lifetime ban on marketing business opportunities. The judgment is reported at $18M, mostly suspended (verify against the order). ([FTC case page](https://www.ftc.gov/legal-library/browse/cases-proceedings/airai))
- **EEOC v. iTutorGroup.** Settled Sept 11, 2023 for $365,000, with EEOC monitoring for at least five years. ([EEOC](https://www.eeoc.gov/newsroom/itutorgroup-pay-365000-settle-eeoc-discriminatory-hiring-suit))

**Defamation and bot-output cases**
- **Walters v. OpenAI** (Gwinnett Super. Ct., No. 23-A-04860-2). Summary judgment for OpenAI May 19, 2025, on three grounds: the output was not reasonably read as fact, there was no negligence or actual malice, and there were no damages. No appeal found (verify). ([AI Lawsuit Tracker](https://ailawsuittracker.com/cases/walters-v-openai-23-a-04860-2/))
- **Wolf River Electric v. Google.** D. Minn. remanded the case to Ramsey County state court on Jan 9, 2026, because Google removed it too late. ([Reason/Volokh](https://reason.com/volokh/2026/01/12/google-missed-key-deadline-in-suit-alleging-googles-ai-libeled-business-court-holds/))
- **Starbuck v. Google** (Del. Super. Ct., No. N25-C-10-211). Motion to dismiss denied July 24, 2026; the case proceeds to discovery. ([Claims Journal](https://www.claimsjournal.com/news/national/2026/07/27/339086.htm))
- **Starbuck v. Meta.** Reported settled Aug 2025, with Starbuck engaged as an adviser on AI bias (verify). ([AI Lawsuit Tracker](https://ailawsuittracker.com/issues/defamation/))

**Chatbot safety cases**
- **Garcia v. Character Technologies** (M.D. Fla., No. 6:24-cv-01903).
  - May 21, 2025 ruling: at the pleading stage, the court refused to treat chatbot output as protected speech and let product-liability, negligence and Florida unfair-practices claims proceed (785 F. Supp. 3d 1157).
  - A settlement in principle was disclosed Jan 7, 2026, on confidential terms. It was part of five Character.AI/Google family settlements.
  - Not confirmed: one source reports that on Aug 4, 2026 the court gave 90 more days. No August 2026 order was found. The January 7, 2026 order (Doc. 244, Judge Conway; T1) dismissed without prejudice and gave the parties 90 days to file a final order or judgment or to move to reopen for good cause; after that period, dismissal becomes with prejudice (about April 7, 2026). No later docket entry was found (check PACER or CourtListener).
  - Sources: [Open Class Actions](https://openclassactions.com/lawsuits/character-ai-google-teen-harm-lawsuits-settlement.php); [Columbia brief](https://law-and-economy.law.columbia.edu/sites/law-and-economy.law.columbia.edu/files/content/The%20Legal%20Risks%20Posed%20by%20AI%20Chatbots.pdf)
- **Other Character.AI claims.**
  - Dismissed with prejudice: the D. Colo. and N.D.N.Y. cases (Oct 15, 2026) and the E.D. Tex. case (Oct 21, 2026).
  - New suit: *A.D. v. Character Technologies* (N.D. Cal., No. 3:26-cv-08400).
  - Character Technologies filed Chapter 11 on Oct 12, 2026 (Bankr. D. Del., No. 26-11603). The automatic stay pauses suits against the company; no stay-scope order was found (T2). Some 30 or more AGs asked the court on Nov 17, 2026 to scrutinize the sale (T2).
  - Sources: [AI Lawsuit Tracker](https://ailawsuittracker.com/ai-chatbot-harm-lawsuits/); [Open Class Actions](https://openclassactions.com/news/character-ai-bankruptcy-chapter-11-lawsuits.php)
- **Raine v. OpenAI** (CGC-25-628528). Coordinated into JCCP No. 5431 on Feb 3, 2026. Case Management Order No. 1 entered Aug 4, 2026. ([AI Lawsuit Tracker](https://ailawsuittracker.com/cases/raine-v-openai-matthew-and-maria-raine-cgc-25-628528/))

**Privacy and wiretap cases**
- **The "Ninth Circuit chat-wiretap appeal (citation unknown)"** is *Gutierrez v. Converse Inc.*, No. 24-4797, 2025 WL 1895315 (9th Cir. July 9, 2025).
  - It is an unpublished, non-precedential memorandum affirming summary judgment for Converse.
  - Holding: there was no evidence Salesforce read or tried to read the chats; proof that it could have was not enough.
  - A concurrence says § 631's first clause does not cover internet communications.
  - Sources: [9th Cir. memo](https://cdn.ca9.uscourts.gov/datastore/memoranda/2025/07/09/24-4797.pdf); [National Law Review](https://natlawreview.com/article/no-wire-no-violation-converses-cipa-win-affirmed-ninth-circuit-court-appeals)
- **Ambriz v. Google** (N.D. Cal., No. 3:23-cv-05437). Orders Oct 22, 2026: Home Depot callers' claim dismissed with leave to amend; callers to Verizon, Uber and Discover sent to individual arbitration (single source, verify). ([AI Lawsuit Tracker](https://ailawsuittracker.com/agentic-ai-lawsuits/))

**Employment, housing and insurance cases**
- **Mobley v. Workday** (N.D. Cal., No. 3:23-cv-00770).
  - July 2025: the collective was expanded to applicants scored by HiredScore.
  - Notice issued Feb 17, 2026; opt-in deadline was Mar 7, 2026.
  - Mar 6, 2026: the court held the ADEA covers applicants.
  - Mid-2026: California-law and disability claims allowed to proceed (date conflicts, verify).
  - July 28, 2026: court refused to order access to applicant data held in customers' Workday systems.
  - Sept 14, 2026: plaintiffs moved to certify four subclasses (race, sex, age, disability). Hearing set Mar 9, 2027.
  - Sources: [Warden AI](https://www.warden-ai.com/warden-watch/litigations/mobley-v-workday-inc); [AI Governance for HR](https://www.aigovernanceforhr.com/p/the-mobley-v-workday-case-didnt-end)
- **Harper v. Sirius XM** (E.D. Mich., No. 2:25-cv-12403). Granted Rule 12(c) motion; dismissed without prejudice Sept 30, 2026 for insufficient facts (ECF 14, T1). ([Warden AI](https://www.warden-ai.com/warden-watch/litigations/arshon-harper-v-sirius-xm-radio-llc))
- **Estate of Lokken v. UnitedHealth** (D. Minn., No. 0:23-cv-03514). In discovery. Discovery order Nov 5, 2026; UnitedHealth expert reports due Jan 25, 2027. ([Georgetown tracker](https://litigationtracker.law.georgetown.edu/litigation/estate-of-gene-b-lokken-the-et-al-v-unitedhealth-group-inc-et-al/))
- **Kisting-Leung v. Cigna** (E.D. Cal., No. 2:23-cv-01477). Active. A 2025 ruling relied on California's rule that only licensed professionals decide medical necessity. ([Georgetown tracker](https://litigationtracker.law.georgetown.edu/issues/artificial-intelligence))
- **Barrows v. Humana** (W.D. Ky., No. 3:23-cv-00654). Aug 15, 2025 ruling let a fraud claim proceed (alleged concealment of AI use). Class certification motion filed Nov 5, 2026; Humana's response due Feb 19, 2027. ([Georgetown tracker](https://litigationtracker.law.georgetown.edu/litigation/barrows-et-al-v-humana-inc/))
- **Louis v. SafeRent.** Settlement is often reported as about $2.275M (not $2.3M), with final approval Nov 2024 (background, verify).

**Copyright cases**
- **Bartz v. Anthropic.** Final approval of the $1.5B settlement July 20, 2026; first payments expected by Nov 15, 2026. Textbook authors objected in Oct 2026 to publishers seeking an 85% share (verify presiding judge). ([Settlement site](https://www.anthropiccopyrightsettlement.com/); [Tracker](https://chatgptiseatingtheworld.com/2026/10/28/academic-textbook-authors-voice-concerns-about-their-publishers-seeking-85-cut-of-bartz-settlement-distribution/))
- **Kadrey v. Meta.** On July 8, 2026 the court refused an interlocutory appeal on the downloading issue. A contributory-infringement claim is in discovery, and new opt-out author suits were assigned to the same judge. ([Tracker](https://chatgptiseatingtheworld.com/category/kadrey-v-meta/))
- **Thomson Reuters v. Ross** (3d Cir., No. 25-2153).
  - Argued June 11, 2026; the panel affirmed for Thomson Reuters in Sept 2026 (exact date: verify).
  - Ross petitioned for rehearing en banc Nov 3, 2026.
  - Sources: [Tracker](https://chatgptiseatingtheworld.com/thomson-reuters-v-ross-intelligence/); [Amici report](https://chatgptiseatingtheworld.com/2026/11/12/amici-briefs-support-ross-intelligences-bid-for-en-banc-review-by-third-circuit/)
- **NYT v. Microsoft/OpenAI.** Now part of MDL No. 1:25-md-03143. Any grounding or output summary-judgment motion is due Nov 25, 2026. ([Tracker](https://chatgptiseatingtheworld.com/2026/11/12/judge-stein-sets-brief-schedule-if-new-york-times-seeks-to-pursue-grounding-and-output-theories-of-liability-v-openai/))
- **Thaler v. Perlmutter.** The Supreme Court denied certiorari around Mar 2, 2026. Works with no human author still get no copyright. ([Tracker](https://chatgptiseatingtheworld.com/2026/03/02/supreme-court-denies-cert-in-thaler-v-perlmutter-declines-to-hear-1st-ai-related-case/))

## 2.17 Messaging, call-recording and AI-voice cases (November 2026 search)

These cases add to Sections 2.3, 2.4 and 2.12.4. Outcomes are split: several courts let CIPA claims against vendors proceed at the pleading stage where the vendor can use the data, and others have dismissed similar claims on third-party, standing or consent grounds (see Sections 2.3 and 2.12.3). The TCPA cases on AI voice and text (*Bradford*, *Bradley*, *Smith*, *Cruz*, *Finley* and *Baus*) remain in Section 2.12.4, and the FCC enforcement actions (*Kramer* and *Lingo*) remain in Section 2.4. The search found no messaging-specific court decisions for Europe, Canada, Australia or Africa. Regulator enforcement in those regions is the more likely source of penalties (verify).

| Case | Court and date | Facts | Outcome or status | Lesson for AI SMS and email | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *Taylor v. ConverseNow Technologies*, No. 25-cv-00990-SI | N.D. Cal. (Judge Susan Illston); order denying motion to dismiss, August 2025 | A caller ordering pizza reached ConverseNow's AI phone assistant. The plaintiff alleges that the call, including name, address and card details, was recorded and intercepted by a third party | Motion to dismiss denied on CIPA Section 631 (capability test for third-party status) and Section 632 (pizza orders can carry a reasonable expectation of privacy). Pleading stage; later status not found (verify docket) | A vendor that can use call data may be a third party under CIPA. Give notice before an AI assistant listens, and limit vendor use of call data | [Duane Morris](https://www.duanemorris.com/alerts/northern_district_california_allows_cipa_claims_against_ai_pizza_ordering_assistant_0825.html); [Squire Patton Boggs](https://aihub.squirepattonboggs.com/2025/12/extra-large-pii-zza-courts-allows-california-privacy-class-action-to-proceed-for-use-of-ai-phone-call-assistant/) |
| *Gladstone v. Amazon Web Services* (Amazon Connect) | W.D. Wash., July 2, 2024; 2024 WL 3276490; 739 F. Supp. 3d 846 | Bank customer calls allegedly recorded through Amazon Connect. The plaintiff alleged that Amazon could use the data to improve its own services | Motion to dismiss denied. The court read "device" in CIPA to include software such as Amazon Connect. Later status not found (verify) | Cloud contact-center vendors can face CIPA claims. Check service terms for any right to use customer data | [Bloomberg Law](https://news.bloomberglaw.com/privacy-and-data-security/amazon-suit-advances-over-recorded-capital-one-customer-calls); [Leagle](https://leagle.com/decision/infdco20240703m74) |
| *Ambriz v. Google LLC*, No. 3:23-cv-05437 (see Section 2.3) | N.D. Cal., February 2025 | Google Cloud Contact Center AI used on customer calls; plaintiffs allege CIPA Sections 631(a) and 637.5 | Claims survived at the pleading stage on the capability theory. Later status to verify | Same lesson as *Taylor* and *Gladstone*: a vendor's capability to use data can be enough | [ZwillGen](https://www.zwillgen.com/privacy/federal-judge-allows-google-customer-service-ai-class-action-to-proceed/) |
| *Popa v. Microsoft* (9th Cir., August 2025) | U.S. Court of Appeals for the Ninth Circuit | Standing to sue under CIPA-type privacy claims over website session recording (facts verify) | Commentary says the Ninth Circuit took a stricter view of standing than the district-court capability trend, which may affect the *Ambriz* trajectory (verify) | Standing defenses may narrow these claims. Do not rely on them when designing consent and notice | [Maine Law SJIPL](https://sjipl.mainelaw.maine.edu/2025/12/18/the-collapse-of-capability-theory-ambriz-popa-and-the-future-of-article-iii-standing-in-ai-privacy-cases/) |

# Part 3. Compliance standards for AI and virtual agents

Standards and frameworks are not laws, but contracts, auditors, card brands, regulators and insurers rely on them. This part covers what each says about AI and virtual agents and what an organization must show.

## 3.1 Payment Card Industry (PCI)

| Standard or document | Status | What it says about AI and virtual agents |
|---|---|---|
| **PCI DSS v4.0.1** | Current (published June 2024; future-dated v4.0 requirements mandatory since March 31, 2025) | Technology-neutral: any chatbot, voice agent, model, transcript store, log pipeline or analytics tool that stores, processes or transmits cardholder data, or can affect the cardholder data environment, is in scope |
| **Integrating Artificial Intelligence in PCI Assessments Guidelines** (v1.0) | Published March 2025 | Assessors may use AI only to support, not replace, human assessors; governs AI use in the assessment process itself |
| **AI Principles: Securing the Use of AI in Payment Environments** | Published 2025 (September per most sources) | High-level principles: AI systems in payment environments must be deployed and managed in line with PCI requirements |
| **Additional guidance on securing AI in payment environments ("Security Considerations for AI Systems")** | Published September 15, 2026 (T1, PCI blog); non-mandatory. The human-approval point in one trade headline is not in PCI's text (unverified) | Protecting data given to AI systems, defending against AI-assisted attacks, and human approval of AI agent actions involving cardholder data |
| **Information Supplement: Protecting Telephone-Based Payment Card Data** | Current version verify | Telephone payments, DTMF masking and suppression, scope reduction, "DTMF bleed" |

**Key PCI DSS requirements applied to virtual agents**

| Requirement | Application |
|---|---|
| Req. 3: never store sensitive authentication data after authorization; render stored PAN unreadable; mask display (first six and last four at most) | No card security code in recordings, transcripts, prompts, vector stores, logs or vendor copies; redact card numbers before they reach the model |
| Req. 4: strong cryptography in transit | TLS for chat, APIs, webhooks and model calls; SRTP for voice; no card data by SMS or email |
| Req. 6: secure development and change control | Prompt, flow and model changes under change control; testing of the bot and plugins; protections for public-facing web apps |
| Req. 7 and 8: least privilege and MFA | Bot service accounts and human agents limited; MFA into the cardholder data environment |
| Req. 10: logging and monitoring | Tool calls and access logged; logs themselves free of card data |
| Req. 11: testing | Penetration testing including AI-specific attacks on in-scope components |
| Req. 12.8 and 12.9: third-party service providers | Written responsibility split with model, telephony, speech and analytics vendors; their attestations reviewed |
| Scope reduction (common practice) | Hand payment to a validated payment page or IVR with DTMF masking so the agent never hears or sees card data; confirm with a QSA |

## 3.2 HIPAA and health-care AI

| Standard or rule | Status | What it requires for virtual agents |
|---|---|---|
| **HIPAA Privacy Rule** (45 CFR Part 164 Subpart E) | In force | Minimum necessary; identity verification before disclosure; patient authorization for marketing; rules for family members and personal representatives |
| **HIPAA Security Rule** (Subpart C) | In force; overhaul proposed January 6, 2025 (mandatory encryption and MFA, removal of "addressable" specifications, annual risk analysis including AI); final rule not issued; target moved to about July 2027 (T2, law-firm reports; verify reginfo) | Risk analysis covering the bot; access control; audit logs; integrity; encryption; contingency plans |
| **Breach Notification Rule** (Subpart D) | In force | Notice to individuals and HHS within 60 days; misdirected messages are a test scenario |
| **Business associate agreements** | In force | A BAA with every vendor that creates, receives, maintains or transmits PHI for the agent (model, speech, telephony, orchestration, analytics, logging, vector database); consumer AI products are usually not covered |
| OCR guidance on online tracking technologies | Guidance; the "Proscribed Combination" portion was vacated in *American Hospital Association v. Becerra* (N.D. Tex., June 2024), HHS withdrew its appeal in August 2024, and the rest remains | Chat widgets must not send PHI to ad or analytics vendors without a BAA or authorization |
| **Section 1557, 45 CFR 92.210** | In force (compliance reported due May 1, 2025) | Identify and mitigate discrimination risk from patient-care decision-support tools, including vendor tools |
| **ONC HTI-1, 45 CFR 170.315(b)(11)** | In force | Certified health IT must support decision-support interventions with source attributes (expanded for predictive interventions trained on data) and risk-management information |
| **CMS Medicare Advantage rule (CMS-4201-F) and February 6, 2024 FAQ** | In force | AI may assist coverage decisions but must consider individual circumstances; length-of-stay predictions alone cannot end post-acute care; no criteria beyond CMS rules; guard against bias |
| **FDA clinical decision support and general wellness guidance** | Final guidance January 2026 | Determines when AI health software is a regulated device |
| 42 CFR Part 2 | In force | Substance-use treatment records |
| State health-data laws (for example Washington My Health My Data Act; California CMIA; state AI-in-utilization-review laws) | In force | Consent and use limits beyond HIPAA, sometimes with private rights of action |
| **HITRUST AI Security Assessment and Certification** | Available since late 2024 (month varies by source) | Up to 44 AI-specific security controls; can be paired with e1, i1 or r2 assessments (whether it can stand alone is described inconsistently; verify); separate AI Risk Management Assessment |
| Joint Commission and Coalition for Health AI guidance on responsible AI use | Guidance (2025; verify) | Governance, monitoring and transparency for hospital AI |

## 3.3 Financial services and insurance

| Standard or rule | Status | What it requires |
|---|---|---|
| **Interagency model risk guidance (SR 26-2; OCC Bulletin 2026-13; FDIC FIL-15-2026)** | Issued April 17, 2026, superseding SR 11-7 | Principles-based model risk management; reported to place generative AI and agents outside its scope, with an AI request for information planned; inventories, validation and monitoring remain good practice |
| Interagency third-party risk management guidance (2023) | In force; principles-based replacement proposed September 2026 (OCC Bulletin 2026-46, verify) | Due diligence, contracts, monitoring and exit plans for AI vendors |
| **ECOA / Regulation B adverse-action notices; CFPB Circulars 2022-03 and 2023-03** | Statute and regulation in force; circulars' status after the CFPB guidance review verify | Specific, accurate reasons for adverse credit decisions, even when AI is used |
| CFPB issue spotlight "Chatbots in Consumer Finance" | Guidance (June 2023) | Chatbots must not trap consumers, give inaccurate information or miss disputes; liability under federal consumer financial law |
| UDAAP; FCRA; GLBA Safeguards Rule | In force | Fair and accurate communications; consumer-report accuracy; security program |
| FINRA Regulatory Notice 24-09 (generative AI) | Guidance (verify current text) | Supervision, communications and recordkeeping duties apply to AI tools |
| SEC books-and-records and marketing rules | In force; the SEC withdrew its predictive data analytics proposal on June 12, 2025 | Retain and supervise bot communications; substantiate AI claims |
| **NYDFS Part 500 (cybersecurity) and October 2024 industry letter on AI cybersecurity risks** | Regulation in force; letter is guidance (verify) | AI-enabled social engineering, AI vendor risk and data protection within the cybersecurity program |
| **NAIC Model Bulletin on insurers' use of AI** | Adopted by about 24 to 25 states plus D.C. | AI governance program, risk management, vendor oversight, documentation on request |
| NYDFS Insurance Circular Letter No. 7 (2024) on AI and external data | Guidance (verify) | Fairness testing and governance for insurers' AI and external data |
| EU DORA; EBA and EIOPA guidance; Consumer Credit Directive 2 | In force or applying (CCD2 from November 20, 2026) | ICT risk, third-party oversight, human review of automated credit assessments |
| UK FCA Consumer Duty and SM&CR | In force | Good outcomes and senior-manager accountability for AI |

## 3.4 Security, privacy and AI management standards

| Standard or framework | Type | What it covers for virtual agents |
|---|---|---|
| **ISO/IEC 42001** (AI management system) | Certifiable standard | AI policy, roles, risk and impact assessment, lifecycle controls, supplier oversight; maps to ISO/IEC 27001 and 27701 |
| ISO/IEC 23894 (AI risk management) | Guidance standard | Risk-management process for AI |
| ISO/IEC 42005 (AI system impact assessment) | Guidance standard (published May 2025; see 3.9) | Assessing impacts on individuals and society |
| ISO/IEC 27001, 27701, 27017, 27018 | Certifiable / guidance | Information security, privacy and cloud controls for the bot platform |
| **SOC 2** (AICPA Trust Services Criteria) | Attestation | Security, availability, confidentiality, processing integrity and privacy, increasingly interpreted to cover AI controls |
| **NIST AI Risk Management Framework and AI 600-1 Generative AI Profile** | Voluntary framework | Govern, map, measure, manage; generative-AI risks and actions including red teaming; referenced as a defense in some state laws (Texas; the original Colorado Act, which SB 26-189 replaced) |
| NIST AI 100-2 (adversarial machine-learning taxonomy) | Guidance | Attack taxonomy, including prompt injection |
| NIST control overlays for securing AI systems (SP 800-53 based) | In development (verify) | AI-specific security controls |
| NIST AI Agent Standards Initiative (February 2026) | Initiative (verify) | Agent identity, authorization and security standards |
| **OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications 2026** (released August 4, 2026) and **Top 10 for Agentic Applications 2026** (December 2025) | Community standards | Prompt injection, sensitive-data disclosure, excessive agency, misinformation, hidden context exposure, goal hijack, tool misuse, memory poisoning, agent supply chain (Section 3.12) |
| MITRE ATLAS | Threat matrix | Adversary tactics against AI systems |
| **UK AI Cyber Security Code of Practice (January 31, 2025)** and **ETSI TS 104 223 / TR 104 128** | Voluntary code and technical standard | 13 principles for secure design, development, deployment, maintenance and end-of-life of AI |
| Cloud Security Alliance AI Controls Matrix | Control framework (verify current version) | Cloud and AI control catalog |
| EU AI Act harmonised standards (CEN-CENELEC JTC 21) | In development | Will give presumption of conformity for high-risk duties |
| Singapore Model AI Governance Framework for Agentic AI (January 22, 2026) | Voluntary | Bounding agent risks, human accountability, technical controls, human approval at irreversible steps |

## 3.5 Telecom and messaging

| Standard or rule | What it requires for virtual agents |
|---|---|
| STIR/SHAKEN (FCC) | Authenticated caller ID for outbound calls |
| CTIA Messaging Principles and Best Practices | Opt-in, STOP and HELP handling, content limits for business texting |
| 10DLC / A2P registration (The Campaign Registry) | Brand and campaign registration; reported 2026 checks on EIN age and opt-in URLs; privacy-policy and terms URLs required by some carriers' platforms from June 30, 2026 |
| Reassigned Numbers Database (FCC) | Check before calling to avoid calling a number's new owner |
| UK Ofcom General Conditions and persistent-misuse policy; TPS and CTPS | Valid caller ID, silent and abandoned call limits, preference-service screening |

## 3.6 Accessibility

| Standard | Status | Application |
|---|---|---|
| WCAG 2.2 AA | Current W3C recommendation; WCAG 2.1 AA is the legal baseline for the DOJ Title II and HHS Section 504 rules (details in Section 3.13) | Chat widgets, streaming replies, keyboard and screen-reader support |
| EN 301 549 | EU and UK public procurement standard | ICT accessibility including two-way voice communication |
| Section 508 | US federal | Federal agencies and their vendors |
| European Accessibility Act | Applies since June 28, 2025 | Accessible customer-service channels |

## 3.7 Content provenance

| Standard | Status | Application |
|---|---|---|
| C2PA / Content Credentials | Industry standard; adoption uneven, audio validation emerging (2026) | Machine-readable provenance for generated media; metadata stripping is a known weakness |
| EU Code of Practice on marking and labelling AI-generated content | Voluntary (June 2026) | Route to EU AI Act Article 50 compliance |
| California AI Transparency Act | In force August 2, 2026 | Detection tools and provenance for covered providers |

## 3.8 Crosswalk: control areas and the standards that require them

| Control area | PCI DSS | HIPAA | Financial | ISO/IEC 42001 and 27001 | NIST AI RMF | OWASP | Laws in Part 1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Keep regulated data out of the model path | Req. 3, scope reduction | Minimum necessary; BAA scope | GLBA | Data controls | Map, Manage | LLM02 | GDPR, state privacy laws |
| Vendor and model-provider oversight | Req. 12.8, 12.9 | BAAs | Third-party guidance, DORA | Supplier controls | Govern | Supply chain | AI Act deployer duties |
| Access control and least privilege for agents | Req. 7, 8 | Security Rule | Part 500 | Access control | Manage | Excessive agency | |
| Logging and auditability | Req. 10 | Audit controls | Books and records | Logging | Measure | | AI Act logging, ADMT rules |
| Testing and red teaming | Req. 6, 11 | Risk analysis | Model validation | Verification | Measure | All | TRAIGA defense, AI Act |
| Human oversight and review | PCI AI guidance on agent actions | CMS, Section 1557 | ECOA, CCD2 | Human oversight | Govern | Excessive agency | GDPR Art. 22, UK 22A to 22D, CA AB 1609 |
| Transparency and disclosure | | | UDAAP | Transparency | Govern | | AI Act Art. 50, state bot laws |
| Incident response and reporting | Incident response | Breach notification | Part 500 notice | Incident management | Manage | | GDPR, CRA, state breach laws |
| Fairness and non-discrimination | | Section 1557 | ECOA, NAIC | Impact assessment | Measure | | NYC LL 144, Illinois, Colorado |
| Accessibility | | | | | | | ADA, EAA, Equality Act |

## 3.9 ISO/IEC and SOC standards in AI regulation

Standards matter legally in five ways: they can create a **presumption of conformity** with a law, support an **affirmative defense or safe harbor**, serve as **evidence of reasonable care** in enforcement and litigation, appear as **contract and procurement requirements**, and determine **who may certify or attest**. Certification is not compliance: no regulator was found that formally accepts an ISO/IEC 42001 certificate or a SOC 2 report as proof of compliance with an AI law, except where the EU cites a harmonised standard.

### 3.9.1 How regulations use standards

| Mechanism | Example | Status | What it means for virtual-agent programs |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Presumption of conformity (harmonised standards)** | EU AI Act Article 40: **EN 18286:2026**, quality management system for providers of high-risk AI (Article 17) | Cited by Implementing Decision (EU) 2026/2274 (in force October 14, 2026; T1); reported cited in the Official Journal through Implementing Decision (EU) 2026/2274 on October 14, 2026 (verify on EUR-Lex). JTC 21 wrote it separately because ISO/IEC 42001's goals and definitions did not match the Act. Risk management (prEN 18228) and logging (prEN 18229-1) drafts were still in approval or comment resolution (one source says some were rejected at enquiry in summer 2026) | High-risk providers can use EN 18286 to presume Article 17 conformity; ISO/IEC 42001 alone does not give that presumption |
| **Affirmative defense** | Texas TRAIGA: substantial compliance with the NIST AI RMF or another nationally recognized AI risk framework | In force January 1, 2026 | ISO/IEC 42001 conformance is likely to support the defense as a recognized framework (verify); keep dated evidence |
| **Rebuttable presumption of reasonable care** | Colorado SB 24-205 (NIST AI RMF or ISO/IEC 42001) | Enjoined or stayed (April 27, 2026), then repealed or replaced by SB 26-189, which does not carry the original presumption (T2; verify text) | Do not rely on the original presumption |
| **Recommended framework** | Washington AI Task Force (NIST AI RMF, ISO/IEC 42001); HB 2157 (high-risk AI) | HB 2157 not enacted (in committee as of February 2026) | Watch for 2027 reintroduction |
| **Disclosure safe harbor (not a standard)** | Utah AI Policy Act | In force | Clear disclosure at the start and throughout, rather than certification |
| **Evidence in sector rules** | HIPAA risk analysis, NYDFS Part 500 vendor oversight, DORA third-party risk, PCI DSS service-provider oversight, CCPA cybersecurity audits | In force | ISO/IEC 27001 certificates and SOC 2 Type II reports are the usual vendor evidence; they do not replace PCI DSS assessments, HIPAA risk analyses or AI-specific testing |
| **Procurement and contracts** | Enterprise RFPs increasingly ask for ISO/IEC 42001 alongside SOC 2 (vendor-reported trend; a cited Gartner figure that 83% of Fortune 500 procurement teams will require 42001 alignment by 2027 is unverified) | Market practice | Expect customers to ask virtual-agent vendors for both |
| **Who may certify or attest** | ISO/IEC 42006:2025 (requirements for bodies certifying ISO/IEC 42001); AICPA standards for SOC reports | 42006 published July 2025 | Accept only accredited certificates; check scope and accreditation |

### 3.9.2 ISO/IEC standards relevant to AI and virtual agents

| Standard | Type | Status | Use |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ISO/IEC 42001:2023** | AI management system (certifiable) | Published; accredited certification available (for example Schellman through ANAB from September 2024; BSI through UKAS and RvA from November 2025 and ANAB from March 2026); IAF CertSearch lists accredited certificates | Governance, risk and impact assessment, lifecycle and supplier controls for AI |
| **ISO/IEC 42005:2025** | AI system impact assessment (guidance) | Published May 2025 | Structure for impact assessments required by the EU AI Act (fundamental-rights assessments), Colorado and CCPA risk assessments |
| **ISO/IEC 42006:2025** | Requirements for audit and certification bodies | Published July 2025 | Confirms a 42001 certifier's competence; now part of ANAB accreditation requirements |
| ISO/IEC 23894:2023 | AI risk management (guidance) | Published | Risk process aligned with ISO 31000 |
| ISO/IEC 22989:2022; ISO/IEC 23053:2022 | Concepts, terminology; machine-learning framework | Published | Shared vocabulary for contracts and policies |
| ISO/IEC 5338:2023 | AI system lifecycle processes | Published | Development and change control |
| ISO/IEC 25059:2023; ISO/IEC TS 25058:2024 | Quality model and quality evaluation for AI systems | Published | Defining and measuring accuracy, robustness and other quality attributes |
| ISO/IEC TR 24027:2021; ISO/IEC TR 24028:2020 | Bias; trustworthiness (technical reports) | Published | Bias testing methods supporting NYC Local Law 144 and fairness tests |
| ISO/IEC TS 6254:2025 | Explainability and interpretability | Published | Explanations for automated decisions (GDPR, CCD2, ECOA) |
| ISO/IEC 5259 series (including 5259-5:2025) | Data quality for analytics and machine learning | Published in parts | Training and retrieval data governance |
| ISO/IEC 12792 | Transparency taxonomy for AI systems | Status conflicting (listed as 2025 by one source; draft with comments to November 3, 2026 by another) | Disclosure and documentation |
| **ISO/IEC 27090** | Guidance on AI-specific security threats | Reported published October 2026; ISO's page still showed it in development (verify) | Prompt injection, poisoning and other AI threats; supports EU AI Act Article 15 and CRA evidence |
| ISO/IEC 27091 | AI privacy guidance | Draft International Standard; publication expected 2026 | Privacy risks in AI and machine-learning systems |
| **ISO/IEC 27001:2022** | Information security management system (certifiable) | Published; transition from the 2013 edition closed in October 2025 (background) | Baseline platform security for virtual-agent infrastructure |
| **ISO/IEC 27701:2025** | Privacy information management system (now standalone and separately certifiable) | Published October 14, 2025; 2019-edition certificates reported to transition by October 2028 | GDPR, UK GDPR and state privacy-law evidence |
| ISO/IEC 27017, 27018 | Cloud security and cloud privacy controls | Published | Cloud-hosted agents |
| ISO/IEC 38507:2022 | Governance implications of AI for boards | Published (background) | Board-level oversight |

### 3.9.3 SOC reports and AI

| Report | Scope | AI status |
|---|---|---|
| **SOC 1** | Controls relevant to customers' financial reporting | Relevant when an agent affects billing, refunds or financial records |
| **SOC 2 Type I and Type II** | Security, availability, processing integrity, confidentiality and privacy (2017 Trust Services Criteria with 2022 points of focus) | No AI-specific criteria in force. AICPA technical Q&A section 9561 (September 11, 2026) explains how AI use affects SOC 1 and SOC 2 examinations without new criteria. On October 28, 2026 the AICPA issued exposure drafts for **2027 Trust Services Criteria** that add AI-related points of focus (reported on 17 criteria, 12 of them common criteria) covering AI governance, model monitoring, prompt manipulation and data poisoning; comments close January 15, 2027; proposed effective date for engagements beginning on or after December 31, 2027, early adoption permitted. A companion Description Criteria draft was reported but not confirmed as posted. A Cloud Security Alliance note (August 2026) said auditors have been improvising AI coverage |
| **SOC 2+** | SOC 2 plus additional frameworks (for example HIPAA, ISO/IEC 42001, NIST AI RMF) | Common way to report AI controls before the 2027 criteria (background) |
| SOC 3; SOC for Cybersecurity | Public summary; entity-wide cybersecurity program | No AI-specific criteria |

### 3.9.4 Regulation-to-standard map

| Regulation or requirement | Standards that provide the strongest evidence |
|---|---|
| EU AI Act Article 17 (quality management, providers of high-risk AI) | **EN 18286:2026** (presumption of conformity once cited); ISO/IEC 42001 as supporting evidence |
| EU AI Act Article 9 (risk management) and Article 27 (fundamental-rights impact assessment) | prEN 18228 (pending); ISO/IEC 23894; ISO/IEC 42005 |
| EU AI Act Articles 12 and 15 (logging, accuracy, robustness, cybersecurity) | prEN 18229 (pending); ISO/IEC 27090; ISO/IEC 27001 |
| EU AI Act Article 50 (transparency) | Code of Practice on marking and labelling; C2PA; ISO/IEC 12792 (when final) |
| GDPR and UK GDPR | ISO/IEC 27701:2025; ISO/IEC 27001; ISO/IEC 27091 (when final) |
| Texas TRAIGA defense; Colorado (original Act) | NIST AI RMF; ISO/IEC 42001 |
| NYC Local Law 144 and other bias-audit rules | Independent bias audit; ISO/IEC TR 24027 methods |
| CCPA risk assessments and cybersecurity audits | ISO/IEC 42005; ISO/IEC 27001; SOC 2 Type II |
| HIPAA Security Rule | HIPAA risk analysis; HITRUST (including the AI Security Assessment); ISO/IEC 27001; SOC 2+ with HIPAA |
| PCI DSS | PCI DSS assessment and service-provider AOC (ISO and SOC reports do not substitute) |
| NYDFS Part 500; DORA; interagency third-party guidance | ISO/IEC 27001; SOC 2 Type II; ISO/IEC 42001 for AI vendors |
| Cyber Resilience Act; NIS2 | ISO/IEC 27001; forthcoming CRA harmonised standards; ISO/IEC 27090 |
| UK AI Cyber Security Code of Practice | ETSI TS 104 223; ISO/IEC 27090 |
| NAIC Model Bulletin (insurers) | NIST AI RMF; ISO/IEC 42001 |

### 3.9.5 Recommendations for virtual-agent programs

1. **Vendors of virtual-agent platforms:** maintain ISO/IEC 27001 and a SOC 2 Type II report; add ISO/IEC 42001 certification from an accredited body; plan early adoption of the 2027 SOC 2 AI points of focus; transition privacy certificates to ISO/IEC 27701:2025.
2. **Deployers:** require vendors' certificates and reports, check that the scope covers the actual agent product and regions, confirm the certifier's accreditation (IAF CertSearch, ANAB, UKAS), and request bridge letters between report periods.
3. **High-risk uses in the EU:** build the quality management system to EN 18286:2026 and track the pending risk-management and logging standards.
4. **Document impact assessments** using ISO/IEC 42005 so one assessment can serve the EU AI Act, Colorado, CCPA and internal governance.
5. **Do not treat certificates as test results.** Standards prove that a management system exists; the corner-case catalog and red-team results prove the agent behaves safely.

## 3.10 Government and public-sector AI compliance (FedRAMP and related)

Virtual agents sold to, or operated by, government organizations face cloud-authorization regimes (FedRAMP, GovRAMP, DoD impact levels), agency AI-use and AI-acquisition policy, and data-specific rules (CJIS, IRS Publication 1075). These apply in addition to the laws in Part 1. FedRAMP does not have a separate AI baseline: an AI service is authorized like any other cloud service, and agencies add AI-specific requirements through policy and contracts.

### 3.10.1 FedRAMP and AI

| Item | Status (verify on fedramp.gov) | What it means for virtual agents |
|---|---|---|
| **FedRAMP Rev5 baselines** (NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5; Low, Moderate, High, LI-SaaS) | In force; machine-readable authorization packages required for all Rev5 providers (RFC-0024, January 13, 2026); one source says Rev5 retires at the end of 2027 (verify) | The whole agent boundary must be authorized: orchestration, model endpoints, speech-to-text and text-to-speech, telephony, vector stores, logging and analytics. Leveraged services (for example the model provider) must hold an authorization at the same or a higher impact level |
| **FedRAMP 20x** | Consolidated Rules for 2026 released June 24, 2026 (T1 changelog); first full Class C 20x certification announced November 2, 2026 (T1); Class A pipeline opened August 3, 2026, Class B and C on August 31, 2026; annual independent assessment of all applicable Key Security Indicators (KSI counts vary by source, for example 56 Low and 61 Moderate); "FedRAMP Ready" retired July 28, 2026 | Evidence is automated and continuous rather than document-based; AI-specific monitoring (model versions, jailbreak and incident rates) fits naturally into KSI evidence |
| **Emerging Technology Prioritization Framework** | Final June 27, 2024 | First priorities were generative AI chat interfaces, code generation and image generation, plus their general-purpose APIs |
| **AI Prioritization** (GSA and FedRAMP, August 25, 2025, at the CIO Council's request) | Closed (initiative completed April 2026; no new entrants; T1) | Prioritizes conversational AI engines for 20x authorization. Reported criteria: enterprise single sign-on, SCIM identity management, role-based access control and real-time analytics; data protection, including that training on customer data does not leave the customer environment without authorization; demand from five CFO Act agencies or a CIO Council recommendation; availability on the GSA Multiple Award Schedule; ability to meet 20x requirements within two months (sources give five or six criteria) |
| Prioritized and authorized AI services | Prioritized list included ChatGPT Enterprise and API, Gemini for Government and Perplexity Enterprise Pro for Government (20x Low targeted for January 2026); vendor sources report further authorizations, including Claude through AWS GovCloud and Google Vertex AI at High (verify on the FedRAMP Marketplace); one June 2026 roundup counted 526 authorized services, 28 under 20x | Check the Marketplace listing, impact level and scope for every model and component the agent uses |
| **Significant change** | Rev5 significant change notification categories (Routine Recurring, Transformative, Adaptive); no explicit FedRAMP rule found on whether a model or version swap is a significant change | Treat model, model-version and major prompt or tool changes as significant changes until the authorizing official agrees otherwise; re-run the agent's safety regression suite before notification |
| Agency authorization to operate (ATO) | Required for each agency | FedRAMP authorization is the reusable baseline; each agency adds AI-specific conditions from OMB policy, its own AI policy and its risk acceptance |

### 3.10.2 Federal AI policy on top of FedRAMP

| Policy | Status | Requirements relevant to virtual agents |
|---|---|---|
| **OMB M-25-21** (federal AI use, April 3, 2025) | In force; deadline for minimum practices on high-impact AI was April 3, 2026, and several agencies reportedly missed it or reclassified use cases | For high-impact AI (for example agents that affect benefits, rights or safety): pre-deployment testing, AI impact assessment, ongoing monitoring for performance and adverse impacts, human oversight and remedies; AI use-case inventories; Chief AI Officers |
| **OMB M-25-22** (AI acquisition, April 3, 2025) | Applies to contracts from about October 1, 2025 (sources differ on the trigger date) | Contract terms on performance monitoring, data rights and intellectual property, privacy, avoiding vendor lock-in, and a preference for American AI |
| **Executive Order 14319** (July 23, 2025) and **OMB M-26-04** (December 11, 2025) | In force; agency policies were due by March 11, 2026; M-26-04 expires December 11, 2027; national security systems exempt but encouraged to comply | LLMs bought by agencies must follow the "Unbiased AI Principles" (truth-seeking and ideological neutrality); vendors must supply acceptable-use policies, model or system cards and a way for users to report outputs that violate the principles; new LLM solicitations must include compliance terms, and vendors can bear termination costs for noncompliance. DoD issued procurement templates in October 2026 |
| **GSA draft AI clause, GSAR 552.239-7001** | Draft released March 6, 2026; comments extended to April 3, 2026; rollout through a Multiple Award Schedule refresh delayed; revised LLM-specific draft in the Federal Register June 17, 2026 (comments 45 days; T1); final text unconfirmed | Would control over vendors' commercial terms, grant broad intellectual-property and irrevocable license rights, include an "American AI" restriction and flow down to service providers |
| **GSA OneGov agreements and USAi** | Agreements with OpenAI, Anthropic and Google extended; first small-business OneGov deal with (US)AI (October 2026, FedRAMP High); CORAS agentic platform agreement through September 30, 2027 (FedRAMP High); GSA planning USAi fees from fiscal year 2027 (reported) | Common route for agencies to buy model access; check that the virtual agent's model endpoint matches an authorized offering |
| **NIST SP 800-53 control overlays for securing AI systems (COSAIS; renamed COSIS on the NIST project page, T1)** | Concept paper August 2025 (overlays for generative AI, predictive AI, single-agent and multi-agent systems, and AI development); first public draft planned in fiscal year 2026, not found published | Expected to become the AI control supplement for FedRAMP and agency ATOs; plan for it |
| NIST Cyber AI Profile | Draft; comments closed January 30, 2026 | Maps the Cybersecurity Framework to AI |
| NIST SP 800-218A (secure development of generative AI) | Published 2024 (background) | Secure software development evidence for model and agent builders |
| NIST AI RMF and AI 600-1 | Voluntary | Basis for agency impact assessments and risk management |
| Privacy Act, privacy impact assessments, systems-of-records notices; Federal Records Act | In force | Agents that collect personal data need privacy documentation; transcripts can be federal records |
| Section 508 | In force | Accessible chat and voice channels |

### 3.10.3 Defense

| Item | Status | What it means |
|---|---|---|
| **DISA Cloud Computing Security Requirements Guide impact levels** (IL2, IL4, IL5, IL6) | In force | Controlled unclassified information generally needs IL4 or IL5; classified needs IL6 |
| **GenAI.mil and DoD AI authorizations** | OpenAI's ChatGPT Mil added August 31, 2026 at IL5; Ask Sage IL5 provisional authorization; Salesforce Agentforce 360 IL5 (reported August 2026); May 2026 agreements to bring several providers' AI into IL6 and IL7 environments; DoD designated Anthropic's Claude a "supply chain risk" in March 2026 (contested; injunction reported, verify) | Confirm the impact level and current DoD status of every model the agent depends on |
| **CMMC** (DFARS rule effective November 10, 2025) | Phase 1 (self-assessments): DoD says they 'remain firmly in place', but its page also says CMMC implementation is paused in Phase 1 (T1, conflicting); Phase 2 (Level 2 third-party assessments), scheduled for November 10, 2026, and all later milestones were **suspended on July 13, 2026**; waiver procedures paused and a CMMC Reform Task Force set up for a 60-day review; no reinstatement reported as of mid-November 2026; Phase 1 still in force | Contractor AI tools that process controlled unclassified information are in scope; cloud services handling it generally need FedRAMP Moderate equivalence under DFARS 252.204-7012 (background) |
