# Appendix A. Status notes from the November 2026 searches

These notes were inside Parts 1 and 2 in version 1.8. They are kept in their original form for audit. Where a note conflicts with a main row, treat the note as the later statement and confirm it against the open items in Part 4.

### Status notes on the state entries above (November 2026)

The rows above have been corrected for Connecticut, Georgia, Idaho, Illinois, Iowa, Maine, Michigan, Nebraska, New York, Pennsylvania, Rhode Island, Tennessee and Virginia. Further notes:

- **Connecticut.** The 2026 chatbot law is SB 5, Public Act 26-15 ("An act concerning online safety"), enacted May 27, 2026.
  - Most sections took effect Oct 1, 2026.
  - AI companion duties (secs. 4-6) start Jan 1, 2027.
  - Employment-technology notice duties (secs. 7-12) apply to technology deployed on or after Oct 1, 2027.
  - AG enforcement only, with no private right of action. A 60-day cure applies to violations through Dec 31, 2027.
  - SB 2 (high-risk AI) was never enacted; it passed the Senate in 2024 and 2025 only.
  - Source: https://www.recordinglaw.com/us-laws/ai-laws/connecticut-ai-laws/
- **Georgia SB 540.** Enacted May 11, 2026; takes effect July 1, 2027. Its duties center on minor account holders: AI disclosure, sexual-content measures, parental tools, and a crisis protocol. Separately, SB 444 (utilization review AI) takes effect Jan 1, 2027.
- **Idaho S 1297.** Enacted Apr 1, 2026; takes effect July 1, 2027 (per LawSignals).
  - The tracker summary says chatbots that are video-game features, customer-service tools or used for business operations are exempt.
  - It says liability is limited to third-party operators of publicly available conversational AI.
  - Confirm against the enrolled text. If accurate, most contact-center bots fall outside it.
- **Iowa SF 2417.** Enacted May 2, 2026; in force July 1, 2026. Operators must disclose AI to minor account holders, either persistently or at the start and every 3 hours. Separately, HF 2635 (utilization review AI) was enacted May 13, 2026.
- **Nebraska LB 525.** Enacted Apr 17, 2026. It combines the Agricultural Data Privacy Act and the Conversational Artificial Intelligence Safety Act.
  - Effective date not confirmed (verify; Nebraska's default is three calendar months after adjournment).
  - The stand-alone chatbot bill LB 1185 died.
- **Rhode Island.** The 2026 chatbot law is HB 7350 (AI companion models), enacted June 22, 2026, effective Jan 1, 2027.
  - It requires crisis protocols and a not-human notice at the start of a session and every 3 hours.
  - Annual reports to the AG start July 1, 2027.
  - Add HB 7349 (AI in mental health care) and HB 7538 (notice of AI visit documentation), both enacted June 22, 2026.
- **Tennessee.** No standalone 2026 companion-chatbot statute was found. The enacted 2026 measures are:
  - SB 1580: no AI may be represented as a qualified mental health professional; in force July 1, 2026.
  - SB 1493 and SB 1700: studies by TACIR, the state's advisory commission on intergovernmental relations. SB 1700 takes effect Jan 1, 2027, with the report due Jan 31, 2027.
  - SB 837: AI is not a "person."
  - HB 1513: political deepfake disclaimer.
  - The LawSignals summary of SB 1493 lists criminal bans on training AI to simulate humans or give emotional support. The enacted caption describes only a study. Do not cite those bans without checking the enrolled text.
  - HB 1898 passed the House only.
- **Michigan SB 760.** Not law. It passed the Senate 20-17 on Apr 29, 2026 and was referred to the House Communications and Technology Committee; no House action found after May 2026. Source: https://www.recordinglaw.com/us-laws/ai-laws/michigan-ai-laws/
- **Pennsylvania.**
  - SB 1090 (SAFECHAT Act; passed Senate 49-1) and HB 2006 (AI Companion Safety Act; passed House 133-70) have each passed one chamber only. Neither is law, and both die Nov 30, 2026.
  - The enacted Pennsylvania AI laws are Act 35 of 2025 (digital forgery) and Act 99 of 2026 (SB 806, AI deceptive advertising, in force about Dec 21, 2026).
- **Virginia.**
  - SB 796 was not enacted. The House Communications, Technology and Innovation Committee continued it to the 2027 session on March 2, 2026, and JCOTS was asked to study it. I found no SB 796-specific JCOTS recommendation. An earlier note said the August 2026 JCOTS report recommends reintroduction; the sources do not support that, so the note is withdrawn.
  - HB 2094 was vetoed Mar 24, 2025; the House sustained the veto Apr 2, 2025.
  - EO 22 (Sept 18, 2026) created an AI task force.
- **New York AI companion laws.** There are two distinct laws:
  - GBL art. 47 (secs. 1700-1704): in force Nov 5, 2025; AG penalty up to $15,000 per day.
  - S9051-B: ch. 368 of 2026, GBL 1800-1805, signed Oct 28, 2026, effective Jan 1, 2027; AG penalty up to $25,000 per violation.
  - Also add the Safe by Design Act (ch. 58 of 2026, part Y; effective Jan 1, 2027).
- **New York RAISE Act.** The original law (ch. 699 of 2025, signed Dec 19, 2025) was repealed and replaced by S8828 (ch. 96 of 2026, signed Mar 27, 2026), effective Jan 1, 2027. Descriptions of the 2025 text are out of date.
- **Illinois HB 2435 (bot disclosure).** Could not be confirmed as enacted. RecordingLaw found no enacted Illinois general AI-disclosure or chatbot law as of Nov 4, 2026.
  - The live bot-disclosure bills are HB 3021 (in House Rules) and SB 317, the Consumer AI Notice Act for customer-service chat, which passed the Senate only.
- **Maine LD 1727.** Enacted June 12, 2025: chatbot disclosure in commercial transactions where a reasonable consumer could be misled. The effective date is presumably 90 days after adjournment (verify). Add LD 2082 (2026, AI therapy).
- **Texas.**
  - HB 1709 never passed; its last action was a committee referral on Mar 14, 2025.
  - TRAIGA is HB 149, signed June 22, 2025, in force Jan 1, 2026.
  - HB 149 also amended the CUBI biometric statute (Bus. & Com. Code 503.001) on consent from publicly posted images.
  - No TRAIGA enforcement action was found as of Nov 4, 2026. The OpenAI suit pleads the Deceptive Trade Practices Act and the SCOPE Act.
- **California dates.**
  - AB 1609: enacted Sept 28, 2026, effective Jan 1, 2027.
  - SB 243: in force Jan 1, 2026. Its minor-user subdivision is deleted by SB 1119 from Jan 1, 2027.
  - SB 942: compliance date Aug 2, 2026 (as amended), then rewritten by SB 1000 (enacted 2026; signing date to verify).
  - SB 7 ("No Robo Bosses"): vetoed Oct 13, 2025, veto sustained.
  - SB 947: signed Sept 30, 2026, with main duties July 1, 2027.
- **Colorado HB 26-1263.** Signed May 29, 2026 per the final fiscal note (the Attorney General's page gives July 1, 2026). Effective August 12, 2026 unless a referendum petition is filed; the Attorney General's page and some secondary sources give January 1, 2027 for operator duties. No petition was found. Also add HB 26-1139 and HB 26-1195 (enacted June 3, 2026; verify).
- **Other enactment dates confirmed.**
  - Hawaii SB 3001: enacted July 14, 2026; annual reports from Jan 1, 2028.
  - Oregon SB 1546: enacted Apr 6, 2026 (Chapter 85); effective Jan 1, 2027.
  - Washington HB 2225: enacted Mar 24, 2026; effective Jan 1, 2027.
  - Washington SB 5395: enacted Mar 23, 2026.
- **Tracker errors to avoid.**
  - LawSignals shows no Minnesota AI law in 2025-26, but Minn. Stat. 325E.91 (2026 ch. 72) exists.
  - LawSignals attributes AI disclaimer and mental-health chatbot rules to Vermont H.814 (Act 101); the enacted text has neither.
  - Kansas has no election-deepfake law; HB 2559 of 2024 failed.

### Status updates to the federal entries above (November 2026)

- **TCPA and FCC 24-17 (AI voice is an "artificial voice").**
  - Since *McLaughlin Chiropractic v. McKesson* (U.S. June 2025), district courts are not bound by FCC interpretations. FCC 24-17 is now persuasive only.
  - The 5th Circuit (*Bradford v. Sovereign Pest Control*, Feb 25, 2026) held the TCPA requires only prior express consent, oral or written. It found the FCC's written-consent rule contrary to the statute.
  - D. Md. agreed in *Bradley* (Mar 20, 2026), but *Smith v. ExamWorks* (Sept 21, 2026) points the other way.
  - Sources: [TCPAWorld](https://tcpaworld.com/2026/02/25/written-consent-new-fifth-circuit-decision-says-congress-never-required-it-in-the-first-place/); [TCPAWorld](https://tcpaworld.com/2026/03/23/massive-ruling-maryland-district-court-joins-fifth-circuit-in-holding-tcpa-does-not-require-written-consent-as-kay-fcc-created-robocall-protections-begin-to-erode/)
- **FCC consent-revocation order (adopted Sept 30, 2026).** A related revocation NPRM was published in the Federal Register. Comments are due Nov 25, 2026 and replies Dec 28, 2026. ([TCPAWorld](https://tcpaworld.com/2026/10/26/on-the-clock-fcc-revocation-nprm-officially-hits-fed-reg-comment-date-november-25-2026-and-reply-due-december-28-2026/))
- **EO 14365, implementation as of Nov 2026.**
  - DOJ AI Litigation Task Force formed Jan 9, 2026.
  - Commerce's evaluation of state laws, due Mar 11, 2026, has not been published (verify).
  - No FCC disclosure-standard proceeding found as of Oct 5, 2026 (verify).
  - FTC proposed AI-accuracy policy statement published in the Federal Register July 7, 2026 (91 FR 41638; comments closed July 31; T1).
  - White House National Policy Framework released Mar 20, 2026.
  - DOJ moved to intervene in *X.AI v. Colorado* Apr 24, 2026, and Colorado AI Act enforcement was stayed by joint motion Apr 27, 2026.
  - Most law-firm sources say Colorado replaced the Act with SB 26-189 (signed May 14, 2026; effective Jan 1, 2027); the enrolled act was not retrieved (verify).
  - Sources: [Holland & Knight](https://www.hklaw.com/en/insights/publications/2026/03/white-house-releases-a-national-policy-framework-for-artificial-intelligence); [Jenner & Block](https://www.jenner.com/en/news-insights/client-alerts/doj-joins-xai-in-lawsuit-challenging-colorado-ai-act); [The Conversation](https://theconversation.com/why-colorado-replaced-its-ai-discrimination-law-with-a-transparency-requirement-that-the-feds-might-challenge-anyway-286517)
- **ECOA and Regulation B.**
  - The CFPB's final rule (published Apr 22, 2026; effective July 21, 2026) removes disparate impact as a theory of liability under ECOA.
  - It narrows "discouragement" to express statements of intent to discriminate and restricts for-profit special purpose credit programs.
  - Disparate treatment, including use of proxies, remains unlawful.
  - The Fair Housing Act and state laws still allow disparate-impact claims.
  - Reported challenge: *NFHA v. CFPB*, No. 1:26-cv-01820 (D.D.C.) (verify).
  - Sources: [Venable](https://www.venable.com/insights/publications/2026/05/cfpb-makes-significant-changes-to-regulation-b); [KPMG](https://kpmg.com/us/en/articles/2026/cfpb-final-rules-regulation-b-Section-1071-and-disparate-impact-liability-reg-alert.html)
- **Fair Housing, Title VII, ADEA, ADA.** Federal disparate-impact enforcement has effectively stopped. HUD withdrew its AI guidance (Sept 17, 2025) and proposed removing its disparate-impact rule (Jan 14, 2026), and EEOC AI guidance was removed (Jan 2025). The statutes and private rights of action are unchanged. ([NAA](https://naahq.org/news/federal-regulatory-changes-seek); [Husch Blackwell](https://www.huschblackwell.com/newsandinsights/ai-and-workplace-discrimination-what-employers-need-to-know-after-the-eeoc-and-dol-rollbacks))
- **CFPB complaint data.** The CFPB stopped discretionary publication of complaint narratives on Aug 14, 2026. Any guidance that tells teams to monitor public CFPB narratives for bot complaints needs updating. ([Columbia brief, fn. 28](https://law-and-economy.law.columbia.edu/sites/law-and-economy.law.columbia.edu/files/content/The%20Legal%20Risks%20Posed%20by%20AI%20Chatbots.pdf))
- **TAKE IT DOWN Act.** The FTC is holding an "Understanding the Take It Down Act" event on Dec 10, 2026. The platform compliance date was May 19, 2026 (background, verify). ([FTC](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2026/07/ftc-seeks-public-comment-policy-statement-addressing-ai-accuracy))
- **Pending bills listed above (GUARD Act S. 3062 and others).** Blackburn's TRUMP AMERICA AI Act draft folds in the GUARD Act's criminal prohibitions. All 119th-Congress bills lapse Jan 3, 2027 if not enacted. ([Holland & Knight](https://www.hklaw.com/en/insights/publications/2026/03/white-house-releases-a-national-policy-framework-for-artificial-intelligence))
- **California CIPA § 638.51 (pen-register) claims.** SB 690, passed August 28, 2026 and reported signed September 30, 2026 by law-firm sources (T2; leginfo not checked), effective January 1, 2027 if enacted, removes private suits under § 638.51 for website and app conduct, including some pending claims. Sections 631 and 632 are unchanged, so contact-center wiretap suits are unaffected. ([DLA Piper](https://www.dlapiper.com/en/insights/publications/2026/10/california-governor-newsom-signs-sb-690); [Privacy World](https://www.privacyworld.blog/2026/11/california-shuts-the-door-on-private-cipa-pen-register-suits-over-websites-leaving-the-closely-watched-variety-media-appeal-in-limbo/))

#### Updates to the summary table in Section 1.10.1

- Brazil PL 2338/2023: still in the Chamber special committee as of October 2026, with no rapporteur report (rapporteur Aguinaldo Ribeiro, chair Luisa Canziani). The vote was pushed past the October 2026 elections after several missed dates. The government still says it wants a 2026 vote, but "2027 vote likely" remains the better estimate.
- Brazil customer service: Decree 13.135/2026 took effect on 2 November 2026 and revokes 11.034. New rules: humans available 8:00 to 20:00 every day on phone and chat; a human option and a cancel option on every menu level; at most five levels; 20-second and 300-second answer-time targets; AI allowed only with a real-time route to a human.
- Brazil telemarketing: the 0303 prefix has not been mandatory since August 2025. Call authentication ("Origem Verificada") is the main tool now, and MP 1.396/2026 adds 15-day blocks for mass short-call traffic from 12 November 2026.
- Mexico "2026 amendments (verify)": the confirmed 2026 change is the 14 May 2026 reform of the Copyright and Labor laws, which requires consent for AI use of performers' voice and image. It is not a general AI law. No federal AI law has passed, and the constitutional reform needed to allow one is still in committee. Also record that the LFPDPPP was replaced on 21 March 2025, INAI was dissolved, and the SABG is now the data-protection authority.
- Peru Law 31814: its regulation (DS 115-2025-PCM) took effect on 22 January 2026. The first private-sector deadline (health, education, justice, security, economy and finance) passed on 10 September 2026, so those obligations are now enforceable.
- Chile Law 21.719: the legal start date is still 1 December 2026, but a bill to move it to 15 October 2027 has passed the Senate and the Chamber Constitution Committee under immediate-discussion urgency. Mark the date as "likely to slip to 15 Oct 2027 (verify final vote)."
- Chile AI bill Boletín 16821-19: in its second constitutional stage in the Senate (the Chamber has already approved it). The executive lowered the urgency to "simple" on 1 September 2026.
- Colombia: add Law 2502/2025 (AI impersonation aggravant, in force 28 July 2026, upheld in C-212/2026) and SIC Circular 002/2024. The government AI bill is stalled in its first debate.
- Argentina "2023 recommendations": these are AAIP Resolution 161/2023 (the program) and the AAIP responsible-AI guide (preliminary, September 2024). Since June 2026 the executive's stated policy has been not to regulate AI.
- Court cases: see Section 2.14. The most relevant to virtual agents are the STF chatbot/robot presumption, ANPD v. Meta, ViaQuatro, SIC v. Worldcoin, and the hallucinated-citation sanctions in Brazil, Colombia and Chile.

#### Updates to the summary table in Section 1.10.2

- Japan, Basic AI Plan: the first plan was adopted by Cabinet on Dec 23, 2025 under Art. 18 of the AI Promotion Act (Act No. 53 of 2025). A revised plan ("Japan AI Transformation") was adopted Jul 14, 2026; the plan is to be updated yearly. Some secondary sources give Apr 14, 2026; ignore that date. Sources: https://www8.cao.go.jp/cstp/ai/ai_plan/aiplan_eng_20260116.pdf ; https://www8.cao.go.jp/cstp/ai/ai_plan/aiplan_eng_20260714.pdf ; https://www.nippon.com/en/news/yjj2025122300289
- Japan, data protection: add the 2026 APPI amendment (passed Jul 10, 2026; promulgated Jul 17, 2026), which introduces Japan's first administrative surcharge. Any statement that Japanese privacy law has no administrative fines is now out of date.
- South Korea, AI Basic Act: the enforcement decree took effect with the Act on Jan 22, 2026. The fine grace period suspends fines but not corrective orders, on-site inspections or suspension orders; officials have said the grace period could be extended. The draft decree's local-representative threshold was KRW 1 trillion in prior-year total revenue (verify against final text). Sources: https://www.cooley.com/news/insight/2026/2026-01-27-south-koreas-ai-basic-act-overview-and-key-takeaways ; https://www.trade.gov/market-intelligence/south-korea-ai-basic-act
- China, anthropomorphic AI measures: reports describe them as the "Provisional Measures on the Administration of Human-like Interactive AI Services", jointly issued by CAC, NDRC, MIIT and MPS, in force Jul 15, 2026 (verify issuing bodies and the Apr 10, 2026 adoption date). Reports say ByteDance and Alibaba removed custom AI companion features as the rules took effect. Sources: https://stdaily.com/web/English/2026-07/28/content_554571.html ; https://thenextweb.com/news/china-humanlike-ai-agent-rules
- China, customer service: add GB/T 47746-2026 (AI and human customer-service coordination, in force Sep 1, 2026). It is voluntary but directly on point for contact-center bots (human handoff, AI labeling, no "false promises").
- Vietnam, data protection: Decree 13/2023 no longer applies. The PDPL (Law 91/2025/QH15) and Decree 356/2025 took effect Jan 1, 2026; the sanctions decree is Decree 330/2026 (Aug 19, 2026). Update any entry that still cites "PDPL 2025/Decree 13" as the current regime.
- Vietnam, AI Law: it is Law No. 134/2025/QH15, passed Dec 10, 2025. One source lists Decree 142/2026 as effective May 1, 2026 and a high-risk list in Decision 33/2026/QD-TTg (46 systems, effective Aug 15, 2026) (both verify, single source). Grace periods for existing systems run to Mar 1, 2027, or Sep 1, 2027 for health, education and finance. Generated audio, image and video must carry visible and machine-readable labels. Sources: https://www.mofo.com/artificial-intelligence/vietnam ; https://www.bakermckenzie.com/en/insight/publications/2026/02/vietnam-artificial-intelligence-law-foundation-and-outlook ; https://blogs.duanemorris.com/vietnam/2026/03/03/vietnam-the-first-law-on-artificial-intelligence-what-you-must-know/
- Taiwan, AI Basic Act: passed third reading Dec 23, 2025; promulgated and in force Jan 14, 2026. Sources disagree on the lead authority (NSTC or Ministry of Digital Affairs); confirm against the final text. Sources: https://moda.gov.tw/en/press/press-releases/18316 ; https://focustaiwan.tw/politics/202512230010 ; https://www.digitimes.com/news/a20251224PD230.html
- Malaysia, AI Governance Bill: public consultation ran Jul 10-31, 2026; the bill was reported complete on Oct 29, 2026 and headed to Cabinet, with tabling planned for early 2027. Source: https://bernama.com/en/region/news.php?id=2627235 ; https://www.mlex.com/mlex/articles/2544364
- Indonesia, presidential regulation: still not signed as of Sep 2026. Komdigi said drafting finished Jul 27, 2026. There are two instruments (AI Roadmap 2026-2029 and AI Ethics), and neither carries penalties. Separately, GR 33/2026 under the PDP Law (in force Jan 16, 2027) should be added. Sources: https://digitalpolicyalert.org/event/42278-ministry-of-communication-and-digital-affairs-announced-completion-of-drafting-of-presidential-regulation-to-govern-artificial-intelligence-utilisation ; https://www.mlex.com/mlex/artificial-intelligence/articles/2506990/indonesia-to-pursue-ai-law-after-presidential-regulation-lays-groundwork
- India, DPDP: Rules notified Nov 13-14, 2025, phased over about 18 months. The penalty schedule is tiered (INR 250 / 200 / 50 crore), not a flat INR 250 crore for every breach.
- Australia, Privacy Act tranche 2: no longer just "proposed". An exposure draft bill was released Aug 31, 2026 (see Laws table). The Dec 10, 2026 date for ADM transparency stands, and the Children's Online Privacy Code shares that deadline.
- Australia, AI guidance: the Voluntary AI Safety Standard was replaced by the Guidance for AI Adoption on Oct 21, 2025. Update any reference to the "10 guardrails" as current guidance.
- Singapore, financial services: MAS final Guidelines on AI Risk Management were published Oct 7, 2026, with phased effect in 2027 and 2028. Treat entries citing the Nov 2025 consultation as superseded.
- Hong Kong, "voluntary guidance": the HKMA generative AI circular and SFC circular are supervisory expectations for regulated firms, and the SFC circular is described as mandatory for licensed corporations. PDPO reform (administrative fines, breach notice) has not been introduced, and the PCPD still cannot fine.

---
## 1.11 AI-sent and AI-received SMS and email, worldwide

This section covers two directions. Outbound: an AVA, a campaign or a call that starts from a message uses AI to draft, pick recipients, send or follow up by SMS or email. Inbound: an AVA receives customer texts and emails, extracts data and acts on them. Almost every regime treats AI as a tool of the business. Consent, sender identity, opt-out, quiet hours and registry checks apply whether a person or a model sends the message, and some regimes add AI-specific disclosure or labeling duties. Status is as of November 18, 2026. Items marked (verify) rest on a single secondary source, background knowledge, or conflicting reports.

### 1.11.1 Core rules that apply in almost every jurisdiction

| Topic | What the rules generally require | Main jurisdictions |
|---|---|---|
| Consent before marketing | Prior express consent (US, where a text counts as a call), opt-in (EU, UK, Germany, Japan, South Korea, Australia, Canada, Colombia, South Africa), or opt-out with narrow exceptions (US CAN-SPAM, Hong Kong, Singapore) | All |
| Sender identity | Accurate sender name, contact details and headers; no disguised or spoofed sender; sender IDs registered where required | US, EU, UK, Canada, Australia, Japan, India, South Africa |
| Opt-out and STOP handling | Opt-out honored promptly, by the same channel or by natural-language request. US CAN-SPAM sets 10 business days; CASL, PECR and POPIA require a working unsubscribe | All |
| AI disclosure | Tell the person they are dealing with AI, or that a voice is artificial, at the start: California B.O.T. Act and AB 2905, Utah, Maine, Colorado and other state chatbot laws, EU AI Act Article 50(1), South Korea AI Basic Act | US, EU, South Korea, others |
| Synthetic content labels | Labels or machine-readable marks on generated content: EU AI Act Article 50(2) and (4), China labeling measures (September 1, 2025), South Korea AI Basic Act | EU, China, South Korea |
| Quiet hours and frequency | Calling windows and contact caps: US telephone solicitation hours (8 a.m. to 9 p.m. local time under the TCPA, applied to texts by many firms; verify), Colombia Law 2300 frequency limits, Australia telemarketing hours | US, Colombia, Australia |
| Registries | Do-not-call and preference lists checked before each campaign: US national and state lists, Australia Do Not Call Register, Singapore DNC Registry, Colombia RNE, India DLT preference registers, UK TPS and CTPS, Brazil and Mexico lists | Multiple |
| Inbound personal data | Lawful basis and notice for processing message content (GDPR Articles 5, 6, 9, 13 and 14); confidentiality of electronic communications; wiretap and CIPA claims when AI listens or records; state privacy laws | EU, UK, US, Brazil, others |
| Decisions made from messages | Notice, explanation and human review for automated decisions (GDPR Article 22; Australia Privacy Act ADM transparency from December 10, 2026; Colorado SB 26-189; NYC rule proposal) | EU, UK, Australia, US states |
| Reassigned and recycled numbers | Check the US Reassigned Numbers Database before contacting; stop sending when a number changes owner | US |

### 1.11.2 United States federal

| Instrument | Status | What it means for AI SMS and email | Possible penalties |
|---|---|---|---|
| TCPA, 47 U.S.C. 227, and FCC rules, 47 CFR 64.1200 | In force | Text messages are treated as calls. Marketing texts and calls sent by an AI system need prior express consent. Whether telemarketing consent must be written is contested after the Fifth Circuit ruling in *Bradford* (February 25, 2026). Consent records should name the technology used (*Bradley v. DentalPlans.com*, D. Md., March 2026; see Section 2.12.4) | USD 500 per call or text; up to USD 1,500 if willful or knowing; private actions, no aggregate cap |
| FCC Declaratory Ruling FCC 24-17 (February 2024) | In force; persuasive after *McLaughlin Chiropractic* (2025) | AI-generated voices are an "artificial or prerecorded voice" for calls. The FCC position on AI-generated texts is less settled (verify) | FCC forfeitures per call (USD 1,000 per call in the 2024 Kramer order; see Section 2.4) |
| FCC NPRM on AI-generated calls and texts (adopted August 2024; Federal Register September 10, 2024) | Proposed; no 2026 adoption found (verify) | Proposed: consent requests must say that consent may cover AI-generated calls or texts; the responsible party must be identified; opt-out rights for AI calls. Outbound only, so inbound customer-service AI is outside it | Not yet in force |
| FCC TCPA consent-revocation order (adopted September 30, 2026) | Effective November 25, 2026 (T1; see Section 1.4) | Callers that do not designate a revocation method must honor any reasonable means within 10 business days, so an AI agent must recognize revocation in free text and route it to suppression. Revoke-all was not adopted; the January 31, 2027 date is superseded (T1) | Per-call TCPA exposure |
| CAN-SPAM Act, 15 U.S.C. 7701 to 7713 | In force | Commercial email, including AI-written email, needs accurate headers, a truthful subject line, a valid postal address, an opt-out honored within 10 business days, and no deceptive sender identity. The business is responsible for mail its vendors and agents send (verify scope of agent liability) | Up to USD 53,088 per email (2025 level, unchanged for 2026; see Section 1.4) |
| Telemarketing Sales Rule, 16 CFR Part 310, and FTC Impersonation Rule | In force | Covers telemarketing calls and sales flows; prohibits misrepresentations, including impersonating a government agency or a business | Up to USD 53,088 per violation |
| FTC Act Section 5 and FTC AI enforcement (Operation AI Comply, 2024) | In force | Deceptive AI claims and fake AI representatives in messages; cases in Section 2.7 | Consent orders; civil penalties for rule violations |
| CFPB chatbot guidance (June 2023) | Guidance; current status to confirm | Wrong answers from a chatbot can breach federal consumer-finance law; bots must reach a human when needed (see Section 1.4) | Consent orders and redress (see Section 1.4) |

### 1.11.3 United States states

| State | Instrument | Status | What it means for AI SMS and email | Possible penalties |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| California | Bot disclosure law (B.O.T. Act), Business and Professions Code 17940 to 17943 | In force since July 1, 2019 | Disclose a bot when it is used online to influence a sale or a vote without disclosure. Whether SMS and email count is not settled (verify) | Enforced through the Unfair Competition Law (verify) |
| California | AB 2905 (2024), Public Utilities Code 2874 (Chapter 316, Statutes of 2024) | In force January 1, 2025 | Automatic dialing-announcing devices that play a voice generated or significantly altered by AI must tell the call recipient. Check the exact disclosure wording against the chaptered text | Enforcement under the Public Utilities Code (verify) |
| California | CIPA, Penal Code 631, 632 and 632.7 (see Sections 2.3 and 2.17) | In force | AI call recording, listening or transcription by a vendor can be interception. Section 632.7 covers calls between cellular and other phones; its reach to SMS is unsettled (verify) | Statutory damages of USD 5,000 per violation (Penal Code 637.2) and private actions |
| California | AB 3030 (2024), generative AI in health-care patient communications | In force January 1, 2025 (verify) | Patient communications generated by generative AI need a disclaimer and a route to a human clinician. Check whether SMS and email are covered | Licensing board action (verify) |
| California | AB 1609, Right to Human Customer Service Act (see Section 1.2) | Enacted September 28, 2026; effective January 1, 2027 | Large businesses must offer a path to a human. Scope for text and email channels to verify | Per Section 1.2 |
| Utah | AI Policy Act, Chapter 13-77, and mental-health chatbot rules, Chapter 13-72a | In force (see the Utah subsection of Section 1.2) | Tell a user on request, and at the start in regulated occupations, that they are dealing with generative AI | Up to USD 2,500 per violation; up to USD 5,000 per violation of an order (Section 1.2) |
| Maine | LD 1727 (2025) | Enacted June 12, 2025 | Using an AI chatbot in trade without disclosure where a consumer could be misled is an unfair trade practice | Per Section 1.2 |
| Oregon | HB 3865 (telephone solicitation, 2026) | In force January 1, 2026 | Covers texts; disclosure and opt-out duties for automatic dialing and artificial-voice calls (see Section 1.2) | Per Section 1.2 |
| Florida | Telephone Solicitation Act (FTSA), amended to cover texts; Security of Communications Act | In force | Prior express written consent for autodialed or prerecorded marketing texts and calls (verify). All-party consent to record calls, which matters for AI recording | Statutory damages per text or call (verify; typically USD 500 to 1,500) |
| Oklahoma, Maryland, New Jersey, Texas and other states | Mini-TCPA and telemarketing laws (see Section 1.2) | In force | Consent, calling hours, registration and texts; some reach AI voice | Statutory damages per call or text (Section 1.2) |
| Washington | Commercial Electronic Mail Act, RCW 19.190 | In force (verify) | Commercial email rules; pleaded alongside the TCPA in *Baus v. Allstate* (November 2026; Section 2.12.4) | Private right of action (verify) |
| Washington | HB 2225 (companion chatbots, 2026) | Enacted; effective January 1, 2027 | Clear disclosure that the bot is not human at the start and periodically | Consumer Protection Act (Section 1.2) |
| Colorado | HB 26-1263, Chatbot Safety Act | Enacted May 29, 2026 per the fiscal note; effective August 12, 2026 unless a referendum petition is filed (none found), with some operator duties from January 1, 2027 (see Section 1.2) | Clear disclosure that a user is interacting with AI | Attorney General consumer-protection penalties (amounts verify) |
| Texas | TRAIGA, HB 149 | In force January 1, 2026 | Disclosure of AI in some health-care interactions (Section 552.051(f)); bans on certain manipulative AI uses | USD 10,000 to 12,000 per curable violation; USD 80,000 to 200,000 per uncurable violation (Section 1.2) |
| New York City | DCWP proposed rule on automated customer service | Proposed October 20, 2026; comment deadline November 19, 2026 per the notice of hearing, but the DCWP rules page lists December 18, 2026 (conflict; confirm with DCWP) | Start of contact must say the consumer is dealing with an automated system, with a route to a human | Proposed (Section 1.9.3) |

### 1.11.4 European Union and member states

| Instrument | Status | What it means for AI SMS and email | Possible penalties |
|---|---|---|---|
| ePrivacy Directive 2002/58/EC, Article 13 (national transposition) | In force | Direct marketing by email, SMS or automated calling systems needs prior consent. The existing-customer exception for similar products applies only where the contact details came from a sale and an opt-out is offered in each message. An AI agent sending marketing is an automated calling system | Set nationally; enforced by data protection and telecom regulators (verify each state) |
| ePrivacy Directive Article 5 (confidentiality of communications) | In force | Protects the confidentiality of electronic communications. It applies to providers of electronic communications services, so check whether an AI vendor reading inbound messages is in scope (verify) | National |
| GDPR, Regulation (EU) 2016/679 | In force | Lawful basis for message content (Article 6); special-category data in messages (Article 9); notice (Articles 13 and 14); absolute right to object to direct marketing (Article 21(2) and (3)); automated decisions (Article 22); DPIA for large-scale AI processing (Article 35) | Up to EUR 20 million or 4% of worldwide annual turnover |
| EU AI Act, Article 50(1) and (2), Regulation (EU) 2024/1689 | In force from August 2, 2026 (Section 1.5) | Tell people they are interacting with an AI system unless it is obvious. Mark synthetic audio and text. The Article 50(2) grace period for systems already on the market runs to December 2, 2026 (sources; verify) | EUR 15 million or 3% of worldwide annual turnover |
| Germany, UWG Section 7(2) Nos. 2 and 3 | In force | Email, SMS and automated-call advertising without prior express consent is unreasonable harassment. Sources say this covers business recipients as well as consumers (verify for calls and texts to businesses) | Injunctions and damages claims; Federal Network Agency (BNetzA) enforcement (verify) |
| France, Code des postes et des communications electroniques L34-5; Bloctel | In force | Opt-in for email, SMS and automated calls; Bloctel for telephone numbers (background, verify) | CNIL and DGCCRF fines (verify) |

### 1.11.12 What the messaging rules mean for virtual-agent design

| Requirement | Build into the virtual agent | Corner-case IDs (Virtual_Agent_Corner_Case_Test_Catalog.md) |
|---|---|---|
| Consent evidence | Record the consent wording, channel, time and the technology named (for example "automated or AI-generated voice or text"). Do not send if the consent record does not name the technology | M3, Q5 |
| AI disclosure | The first message of every SMS or email thread, and the opening of every AI voice call, states that an AI is involved. Repeat in long threads. Answer "Am I talking to a bot?" truthfully | M1, G-series |
| Opt-out | Recognize STOP, UNSUBSCRIBE, REVOKE and natural-language requests in every supported language. Apply to all channels and campaigns before the next send | M4, Q4 |
| Hours, frequency and locale | Send only in the recipient's local window. Enforce frequency caps (Colombia, US states, Brazil) | M3, M6, Q6 |
| Sender identity | Accurate sender name, number and domain. No AI persona posing as a named human agent. SPF, DKIM and DMARC in place; postal address in email; registered sender IDs where required | Q1, M1 |
| Registry scrub | Check DNC, RNE, TPS, CTPS, the Australian Do Not Call Register and the Reassigned Numbers Database before each campaign. Fail closed if a check is unavailable | M3 |
| Inbound messages as data | Treat instructions inside texts and emails as data, not commands. Restrict tools. Do not act on sender claims without verification. Check DMARC results on email | J2, J4, J9, category I |
| Sensitive content in messages | Detect health, financial and minors' data. Minimize and retain only as needed. Give notice before any voice recording | H and L categories, M2, Q3 |
| Decisions from messages | Refunds, eligibility, fraud holds and credit changes: notice, explanation and human review where required | M5 |
| Synthetic-content labels | Watermark or label generated media and text where EU, Chinese or Korean rules apply | Q2 |
| Pre-launch checks | Seed-list tests for headers, opt-out, disclosure and carrier filtering in each market | M3, M4, P1 |

### 1.11.13 Patterns across jurisdictions

Four patterns matter most for AVA design. First, consent and registry checks come before the first message, and in several jurisdictions consent must name the technology used. Second, AI disclosure is spreading from chat to voice and text. Third, inbound message content is treated as personal data, and listening to or recording it can trigger wiretap-style claims in California and elsewhere. Fourth, decisions taken from messages face notice and review duties in the EU, the UK, Australia from December 10, 2026, and several US states. A single consent, disclosure and opt-out layer shared across channels meets most of these rules at once. Local registries, calling hours and sender-ID rules then need to be added market by market.
